Duality for the condensed Weil-étale realisation of 1 1 -motives over p p -adic fields
Marco Artusa , with an appendix written by Takashi Suzuki

Abstract
We extend Tate duality for Galois cohomology of abelian varieties to 1 1 -motives over a p p -adic field, improving a result of Harari and Szamuely. To do this, we replace Galois cohomology with the condensed cohomology of the Weil group. This is a topological cohomology theory defined in a previous work, which keeps track of the topology of the p p -adic field. To see 1 1 -motives as coefficients of this cohomology theory, we introduce their condensed Weil-étale realisation. Our duality takes the form of a Pontryagin duality between locally compact abelian groups.



1
Introduction

Duality theorems are among the central statements in arithmetic geometry. For a
p
-adic field
F
, the earliest example is due to Tate
[
32
]
, who proves a duality theorem for Galois cohomology of abelian varieties. His result can be stated as follows

Theorem A
.

Let
A
be an abelian variety over a
p
-adic field
F
and let
A^{*}
be its dual abelian variety. For
q=0,1
, there is a perfect pairing

\mathrm{H}^{q}(G_{F},A(\overline{F}))\times\mathrm{H}^{1-q}(G_{F},A^{*}(\overline{F}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(G_{F},\overline{F}^{\times})=\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}

between a profinite abelian group and a torsion abelian group.

The goal of this paper is to extend this result to
1
-motives replacing Galois cohomology with the condensed cohomology of the Weil group, developed in
[
3
]
. Indeed, such a generalisation in terms of Galois cohomology is not satisfying enough. The reason is that completion procedures are needed on cohomology groups to make the paring perfect (see for example
[
23
, Corollary 2.3]
and
[
15
, Theorem 0.1]
), which lose information on the original groups. In our new framework, the duality for
1
-motives is expressed as a Pontryagin duality between locally compact abelian groups and no completion procedure is needed.

Let
F
be a
p
-adic field. Certain shortcomings of Galois cohomology for duality theorems have been observed in
[
3
]
, where a new cohomology theory is provided by replacing the Galois group
G_{F}
with the Weil group
W_{F}
and the category of sets with the category
\mathscr{C}
of condensed sets. The resulting cohomology groups
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},-)
are condensed abelian groups, and the category of coefficients, denoted by
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
, is the category of condensed abelian groups with an action of the pro-condensed group
\hat{W}_{F}
. Since
\mathscr{C}
contains compactly generated topological spaces as a full subcategory stable by all limits, both the coefficients and the cohomology groups are naturally topologised.

In this paper, we associate to a
F
-commutative group scheme locally of finite type, say
G
, a condensed abelian group with an action of
\hat{W}_{F}
. We informally present this construction. Let
L
be the completion of the maximal unramified extension
F^{\mathrm{un}}
. Let us fix separable closures
\overline{F}\subset\overline{L}
of
F
and
L
respectively. The Weil group
W_{F}
acts continuously on the abelian group
G(\overline{L})
. We observe that
\overline{L}
has a natural topology induced by the one of
L
, which comes from the non-archimedean topology on
F
. Then
G(\overline{L})
can be seen as a topological abelian group with the topology induced by the one of
\overline{L}
. This operation gives
G(\overline{L})
the structure of a condensed abelian group with an action of
\hat{W}_{F}
. We denote this object
G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
and we call it
condensed Weil-étale realisation
of
G
. We consider the cohomology groups
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
, condensed abelian groups which keep track of the topology of
F
. The underlying abelian groups coincide with the cohomology groups
\mathrm{H}^{q}(W_{F},G(\overline{L}))
considered by Karpuk in
[
18
]
.

By functoriality, we extend this construction to
1
-motives. A
1
-motive over
F
is a complex of
F
-commutative group schemes concentrated in degrees
-1
and
0
, say
\mathcal{M}=[Y\to E]
, where
Y
is étale-locally isomorphic to the constant group
\mathbbm{Z}^{r}
and
E
is a semiabelian variety, i.e. an extension of an abelian variety by a torus. Following
[
9
]
, we associate to
\mathcal{M}
a Cartier dual
\mathcal{M}^{*}=[Y^{*}\to E^{*}]
. We consider the condensed Weil-étale realisations
\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
and we study the total derived cohomologies
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
, showing that they are represented by bounded complexes of locally compact abelian groups. We define the
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
-twist of the condensed cohomology of
\mathcal{M}^{*}
, and we denote it
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
. Using a condensed version of biextensions, we build a cup-product pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

(1.1)

Our main result can be stated as follows

Theorem
4.21
.

The cup-product pairing (
1.1
) is perfect. Moreover, it induces perfect cup-product pairings of locally compact abelian groups

\begin{split}\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{lca}}&\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},\\
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})&\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},\\
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})&\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}\\
\end{split}

and a perfect pairing

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{nh}}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

We observe the presence of 4 pieces contributing to the cohomology of
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
. Indeed, the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is not locally compact and can be decomposed in a maximal locally compact quotient
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}
and a non-Hausdorff subgroup
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}
. The same holds for
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
and, more generally, for every object of the left heart of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
, the bounded derived category of the quasi-abelian category
\mathbf{LCA}
of locally compact abelian groups.

If
\mathcal{M}=[0\rightarrow A]
is an abelian variety, the underlying abelian groups of
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
and
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
coincide with
\mathrm{H}^{q}(G_{F},A(\overline{F}))
and
\mathrm{H}^{q-1}(G_{F},A^{*}(\overline{F}))
respectively. Thus our result is a topological improvement of Theorem
A
. Indeed, Tate’s cohomology groups are abelian groups, hence they are not naturally topologised, contrarily to our condensed cohomology groups. Nonetheless, Tate’s duality for abelian varieties is a key ingredient to prove our result. If
\mathcal{M}=[0\rightarrow T]
is a torus, our result is a condensed-Weil version of Tate-Nakayama duality which does not need profinite completion, and is a consequence of
[
3
, Theorem 4.27]
. In general, our result is an improved version of
[
15
, Theorem 0.1]
in terms of the Weil group and of Condensed Mathematics.

1.1
Relation with previous work

Let us recall existing generalisations of Theorem
A
and their shortcomings, starting from the setting of Galois cohomology. Let
T
be a torus over
F
and let
Y^{*}
be its Cartier dual. Cartier duality induces a cup-product pairing

\mathrm{H}^{q}(G_{F},Y^{*}(\overline{F}))\times\mathrm{H}^{2-q}(G_{F},T(\overline{F}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(G_{F},\overline{F}^{\times})=\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}.

(1.2)

To generalise Theorem
A
to tori, one would expect this pairing to be perfect. However, this does not happen. Indeed, if
T=\mathbbm{G}_{m}
and
q=0
, then (
1.2
) becomes the pairing

\mathbbm{Z}\times\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z},

which cannot be perfect since the induced map
\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow\Hom(\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z},\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})=\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}
is not an isomorphism. However, we recover perfectness of the pairing if we replace the
0
th cohomology group with its profinite completion. This result is called Tate-Nakayama duality and can be stated as follows (see for example
[
23
, Corollary 2.3]
)

Theorem B
.

The pairing (
1.2
) becomes perfect if in the case
q\neq 1
we replace
\mathrm{H}^{0}
with its profinite completion.

If
T=\mathbbm{G}_{m}
and
q=2
we recover the reciprocity isomorphism of local class field theory

(F^{\times})^{\wedge}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}G_{F}^{ab}.

This completion procedure loses information on the original groups. Moreover, since it is not an exact operation, it causes further issues when we want to generalise Theorems
A
and
B
to complexes of group schemes, e.g.
1
-motives.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\rightarrow E]
be a
1
-motive over
F
and let
\mathcal{M}^{*}
be its dual
1
-motive. The Poincaré biextension gives a cup-product pairing on Galois cohomology

R\Gamma(G_{F},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(G_{F},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{F}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(G_{F},\overline{F}^{\times})[-1]=\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1].

(1.3)

Harari and Szamuely prove the following duality result (see
[
15
, Theorem 0.1]
)

Theorem C
.

The cup-product pairing (
1.3
) induces perfect pairings

\mathrm{H}^{-1}(G_{F},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F}))_{\wedge}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{2}(G_{F},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{F}))\rightarrow\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}

and

\mathrm{H}^{0}(G_{F},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F}))^{\wedge}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}(G_{F},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{F}))\rightarrow\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}.

Here
\mathrm{H}^{0}(G_{F},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F}))^{\wedge}
is the profinite completion of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(G_{F},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F}))
, while
\mathrm{H}^{-1}(G_{F},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F}))_{\wedge}
is the kernel of
Y(F)^{\wedge}\rightarrow E(F)^{\wedge}
.

The need of two different completion procedures
(-)^{\wedge}
and
(-)_{\wedge}
comes from the lack of exactness of profinite completion that we mentioned before. Generalisations of Theorem
A
should not involve such completion procedures.

In
[
18
]
, Karpuk tries to solve this issue by using the Weil group
W_{F}
instead of the Galois group, following Lichtenbaum’s intuition
[
19
]
. If
G
is a commutative group scheme over
F
, and
L
is the completion of the maximal unramified extension of
F
, we have a natural action of
W_{F}
on
G(\overline{L})
and we can consider the cohomology
R\Gamma(W_{F},G(\overline{L}))
. If
T
is a torus over
F
with Cartier dual
Y^{*}
, we obtain a pairing

R\Gamma(W_{F},Y^{*}(\overline{L}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(W_{F},T(\overline{L}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{F},\overline{L}^{\times})[-1]=\mathbbm{Z}[-1].

(1.4)

Karpuk’s version of Theorem
B
using the Weil group can be stated as follows (see
[
17
, Theorem 3.3.1]
)

Theorem D
.

The cup-product pairing (
1.4
) induces an equivalence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab})

R\Gamma(W_{F},T(\overline{L}))\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}R\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}}(R\Gamma(W_{F},Y^{*}(\overline{L})),\mathbbm{Z}[-1]).

This is a duality theorem for tori which does not need profinite completion to hold. It is a significant improvement, but it still encounter certain limitations. First of all, it is a duality theorem which is expressed only at the level of derived categories. Since cohomology groups of
R\Gamma(W_{F},Y^{*}(\overline{L}))
may have non-trivial torsion, this result does not directly induce a duality between cohomology groups. Moreover, the other map induced by (
1.4
)

R\Gamma(W_{F},Y^{*}(\overline{L}))\rightarrow R\Hom(R\Gamma(W_{F},T(\overline{L})),\mathbbm{Z}[-1])

is not an equivalence in general (see
[
17
, Proposition 3.3.8]
). Thus the pairing (
1.4
) is not perfect. In the same spirit, Karpuk’s version of Theorem
A
does not recover a perfect pairing on the cohomology of the Weil group. Indeed, if
A
is an abelian variety over
F
and
A^{*}
is its dual abelian variety, we have a cup-product pairing

R\Gamma(W_{F},A(\overline{L}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(W_{F},A^{*}(\overline{L}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{F},\overline{L}^{\times})=\mathbbm{Z}

and an induced morphism

\tau(A):R\Gamma(W_{F},A^{*}(\overline{L}))\rightarrow R\Hom(R\Gamma(W_{F},A(\overline{L})),\mathbbm{Z}).

Karpuk’s result is stated as follows (see
[
18
, Theorem 5.4.4]
)

Theorem E
.

The map
\tau(A)
has the following properties:

(i)

\tau^{0}(A):A^{*}(F)\rightarrow\Ext(\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{F},A(\overline{L})),\mathbbm{Z})=\Ext(\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{F},A(\overline{L})),\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})
is an isomorphism of profinite groups.

(ii)

\tau^{1}(A):\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{F},A^{*}(\overline{L}))\rightarrow\Ext(A(F),\mathbbm{Z})
induces an isomorphism of
\mathrm{H}^{1}(W_{F},A^{*}(\overline{L}))
with the torsion subgroup
\Hom(A(F),\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})
of
\Ext(A(F),\mathbbm{Z})
.

Even though we are still able to recover Tate’s duality for abelian varieties, the cup-product pairing inducing
\tau(A)
is not perfect.

Let us point out a deeper explanation of why Karpuk’s improvements are not optimal. The reason why the profinite completion appears in duality theorems for Galois cohomology is the following. Such results are expressed in terms of
discrete
Pontryagin duality

\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}}(-,\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}):\mathbf{Ab}^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab},

which realises an antiequivalence of categories between profinite abelian groups and torsion abelian groups, but it is
not
an antiequivalence of categories on the whole category
\mathbf{Ab}
. To make the cup-product pairings perfect one needs to work with profinite and torsion abelian groups and this is why profinite completions appear in Theorems
B
and
C
. In Karpuk’s setting of the cohomology of
W_{F}
, the results are expressed in terms of
\mathbbm{Z}
-linear duality

R\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}}(-,\mathbbm{Z}):\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}),

which is not an antiequivalence of categories either. The pairings involved in Theorems
D
and
E
are not perfect since the complexes
R\Gamma(W_{F},A(\overline{L}))
and
R\Gamma(W_{F},Y^{*}(\overline{L}))
are not dualisable. To fix this, we consider the
topological
Pontryagin duality

R\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{LCA}}(-,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}):\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})

which is an antiequivalence of categories. This suggests that to have satisfying duality theorems, our cohomologies should be naturally topologised, as remarked by Geisser and Morin in
[
10
]
. Condensed Mathematics
[
7
,
4
]
provide a topos
\mathscr{C}
of condensed sets which contains compactly generated abelian groups as a full subcategory stable by all limits. Moreover, the bounded
\infty
-derived category of condensed abelian groups
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\mathscr{C})
contains
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\mathbf{LCA})
as a full stable
\infty
-subcategory (see
[
3
, Lemma 4.2]
). To express our duality theorems as a topological Pontryagin duality, we want a cohomology theory which takes values in
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\mathscr{C})
.

After the first appearence of this article on the web, it was drawn to the attention of the author that Suzuki proved a duality theorem for
1
-motives in this spirit in
[
28
]
. Indeed, using the so-called ind-rational pro-étale site of the residue field
k
, denoted by
\mathrm{Spec}(k^{\mathrm{indrat}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
, he builds a cohomology theory
R\Gamma(F_{W},-)
with values in
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\overline{k}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})=\mathbf{D}^{b}(\mathscr{C})
, having complexes of fppf sheaves as coefficients. For a
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}
, the underlying complex of abelian groups
R\Gamma(F_{W},\mathcal{M})(*)
coincides with the cohomology of the Weil group. He then proves the following (see
[
28
, Section 10]
)

Theorem F
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a
1
-motive over
F
with Cartier dual
\mathcal{M}^{*}
. We have a perfect cup-product pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\mathscr{C})

R\Gamma(F_{W},\mathcal{M})\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(F_{W},\mathcal{M}^{*})\longrightarrow\mathbf{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(F_{W},\mathbbm{G}_{m})=\mathbbm{Z}.

This result is a condensed (and
\mathbbm{Z}
-linear) version of Theorem
C
and does not need profinite completion to hold. In this sense, it is certainly an improvement of both Theorem
C
and of Theorems
D
and
E
. Moreover, it has the great merit of being stated as a condensed duality before the formalisation of the theory by Clausen-Scholze and Barwick-Haine (see the original version
[
29
]
). However, contrarily to Harari and Szamuely’s result, it is a duality only on the derived category level. Similarly to Theorem
D
, a concrete expression of the duality on each cohomology object is missing.

In this paper, we use a different and independent approach which builds on the results of
[
3
]
, where the need for a topological cohomology theory is addressed by using the topos
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
, the classifying topos of
W_{F}
seen as a pro-object of
\mathbf{Grp}(\mathscr{C})
. Our cohomology theory is denoted by
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},-)
and takes values in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
. In this way we solve the issues of the classical Tate approach (i.e. the profinite completion) and of Karpuk’s approach (i.e. the lack of topology). The analogue of Theorem
F
can still be recovered in our setting (see
Theorem

4.20
). Moreover, we carry out a deeper study of the structure of each cohomology group (
Section

3.4
) and of the Pontryagin duality in
\mathbf{D}^{b}(\mathbf{LCA})
(
Section

4.1
). Thanks to this, we prove our main result (
Theorem

4.21
), which is a condensed duality for the cohomology of
1
-motives expressed as a Pontryagin duality on each cohomology group. Thus, it contains Theorem
A
and improves simultaneously Theorems
B
,
C
,
D
,
E
and
F
.

We conclude this section by saying a few words on the relation between the results of this work and the ones of
[
3
]
. Both
Theorem

4.21
and
[
3
, Theorem 4.27]
extend Tate’s local duality to more general coefficients.

Theorem G

(Tate local duality)
.

Let
N
be a finite abelian group with a continuous action of
G_{F}
. Then we have a perfect cup-product pairing

\mathrm{H}^{q}(G_{F},N)\otimes\mathrm{H}^{2-q}(G_{F},\Hom(N,\overline{F}^{\times}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(G_{F},\overline{F}^{\times})=\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}.

The difference is that
[
3
, Theorem 4.27]
extends such coefficients in a
topological
direction: from finite discrete abelian groups to some locally compact abelian groups including
\mathbbm{Z}^{n}
with the discrete topology, countable abelian groups with the discrete topology,
\mathbbm{R}^{n}
with the Euclidean topology,
(\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{n}
with its compact Hausdorff topology.
Theorem

4.21
extends the coefficients in a
algebro-geometric
direction: from finite group schemes to abelian varieties, tori, and more generally
1
-motives. To do this, the new tool of condensed Weil-étale realisation is fundamental, allowing to see algebro-geometric objects as coefficients of the condensed cohomology of
\hat{W}_{F}
.

1.2
Outline

In
Section

2
we introduce the condensed Weil-étale realisation. This is a functor which associates to a scheme
X
which is locally of finite type over a
p
-adic field
F
an object of
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
, i.e. a condensed set with an action of the pro-condensed group
\hat{W}_{F}
. This functor respects fiber products. In particular, if
G
is a commutative
F
-group scheme locally of finite type, then its condensed Weil-étale realisation
G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is an object of
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
. We also show that this functor respects the exactness of some exact sequences

Corollary
2.11
.

Let

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow 0

be an exact sequence of commutative algebraic groups over
F
. Suppose that for every finite field extension
K
of
L
the topological abelian groups
A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
and
A^{\prime\prime}(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
are prodiscrete. Then the corresponding sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow 0.

is exact.

By functoriality, we extend this construction to
1
-motives. If
\mathcal{M}=[Y\rightarrow E]
is a
1
-motive over
F
, we define its condensed Weil-étale realisation
\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\coloneqq[Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}]
, which represents an object of the derived category
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
. As a consequence of
Corollary

2.11
we prove the existence of a condensed weight filtration

Proposition
2.14
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
be a 1-motive over
F
. We have a filtration

0\hookrightarrow W_{-2}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\hookrightarrow W_{-1}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\hookrightarrow W_{0}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}

with
W_{-2}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\coloneqq[0\rightarrow T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}]
,
W_{-1}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\coloneqq[0\rightarrow E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}]
. Moreover, we have

\begin{split}gr^{0}\coloneqq W_{0}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}/W_{-1}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}&=[Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow 0],\\
gr^{-1}\coloneqq W_{-1}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}/W_{-2}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}&=[0\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}],\\
\end{split}

In
Section

3
we study the structure of
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
when
\mathcal{M}
is a
1
-motive over
F
. We start with the case where
\mathcal{M}
is either a torus (see
Proposition

3.2
), an abelian variety (see
Proposition

3.9
) or a semiabelian variety (see
Proposition

3.11
). In all these cases the cohomology groups
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
are represented by locally compact abelian groups (of finite ranks) and vanish if
q\neq 0,1
. In general we cannot expect
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
to be represented by a locally compact abelian group. For exemple, we set
\mathcal{M}=[\mathbbm{Z}\overset{u}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}]
, where we have

u(\mathbbm{Q}_{p}):\mathbbm{Z}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{Q}_{p}^{\times},1\mapsto 1+p.

Under the identification
\mathbbm{Q}_{p}^{\times}\cong\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\oplus\mathbbm{F}_{p}^{\times}\oplus\mathbbm{Z}
, the image of
u(\mathbbm{Q}_{p})
is a dense subset of
\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
. Then
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
contains a non-Hausdorff subgroup
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}\coloneqq\mathbbm{Z}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}
. More generally, we prove the following

Theorem
3.14
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
be a 1-motive over
F
. Then
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is represented by a bounded complex of locally compact abelian groups of finite ranks. Moreover, we have

•

\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is a discrete free finitely generated abelian group.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is an extension of
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}
by
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}
.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}
is the cokernel of a dense injective morphism of locally compact abelian groups of finite ranks (non-Hausdorff subgroup).

•

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}
is a locally compact abelian group of finite ranks.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is discrete of finite ranks.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=0
for all
q\neq 0,\pm 1
.

Moreover, we define the
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
-twisted cohomology
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
. In the case where
\mathcal{M}
is either an abelian variety, a torus or a
F
-group scheme étale-locally isomorphic to
\mathbbm{Z}^{r}
, we also establish the relation between
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
and
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
for all
q
. This is done in
Proposition

3.20
. Informally speaking, copies of
\mathbbm{Z}
in
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
are replaced by copies of
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
in degree
q
, while the rest is moved to degree
q-1
cohomology.

In
Section

4
we state and prove duality theorems for the condensed cohomology of the Weil group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},-)
with coefficients in condensed Weil-étale realisations of tori, abelian varieties and, more generally,
1
-motives. Our dualities are expressed in terms of Pontryagin duality between complexes in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, the bounded derived
\infty
-category of the quasi-abelian category of locally compact abelian groups of finite ranks (see
[
10
, 2.2]
). As explained in
[
3
, 4.1]
, this is a full stable
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
.

First, we introduce a canonical decomposition in the left heart
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{LCA})
. We define a full subcategory
\mathcal{NH}\subset\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{LCA})
of
non-Hausdorff
objects. Moreover, for all
M\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{LCA})
, we show the existence of a unique (up to isomorphism) exact sequence

0\rightarrow M^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow M\rightarrow M^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0,

with
M^{\mathrm{nh}}\in\mathcal{NH}
and
M^{\mathrm{lca}}\in\mathbf{LCA}
. This is the analogue in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{LCA})
of the decomposition of a finitely generated abelian group
A

0\rightarrow A_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow A\rightarrow A_{\mathrm{free}}\rightarrow 0.

We use this decomposition to better understand Pontryagin duality in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
. Indeed, we prove the following

Lemma
4.4
.

Let
C,D\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
be two objects in the essential image of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})\to\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
. Suppose that we have a perfect pairing

C\otimes^{L}D\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

Then for all
n\in\mathbbm{N}
we have induced perfect pairings in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})

\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{lca}}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}

and perfect pairings in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})

\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{nh}}\otimes^{L}\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D)^{\mathrm{nh}}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

The first pairings identify
\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{lca}}
with
\underline{\Hom}(\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)^{\mathrm{lca}},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
and viceversa, while the second ones identify
\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{nh}}
with
\underline{\Ext}(\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D)^{\mathrm{nh}},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
and viceversa. All these results hold when
\mathbf{LCA}
is replaced with
\mathbf{FLCA}
.

Duality for the condensed Weil-étale realisation of tori is treated in
Section

4.2
. If
T
is a torus over
F
and
Y^{*}
is its Cartier dual, we show the existence of a cup-product pairing

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},Y^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times})[-1]=\mathbbm{Z}[-1].

(1.5)

We prove the following

Proposition
4.14
.

The cup-product pairing (
1.5
) is a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
.

In
Section

4.3
, we deal with duality for condensed Weil-étale realisations of abelian varieties. Let
A
be an abelian variety over
F
and
A^{*}
its dual abelian variety. Using a condensed version of the theory of biextensions, which is developed in the Appendix
A
, we define a condensed Poincaré pairing

\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\otimes^{L}A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\overline{L}^{\times}[1]

(1.6)

and the induced cup-product pairing

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W_{F}}},\overline{L}^{\times})[0]=\mathbbm{Z}.

(1.7)

We prove the following

Theorem
4.19
.

(Tate duality for abelian varieties)
The cup-product pairing (
4.7
) is a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
and factors through
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1]
. In particular, for
q=0,1
, we get a perfect pairing

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{1-q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

between a profinite abelian group (of finite ranks) and a torsion abelian group (of finite ranks).

We generalise both results to
1
-motives. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a
1
-motive over
F
and
\mathcal{M}^{*}
its dual
1
-motive. As in the case of abelian varieties, we use the condensed theory of biextensions to define a condensed Poincaré pairing

\psi_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\overline{L}^{\times}[1]

(1.8)

and a cup-product pairing

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

(1.9)

We prove our main result, which we already stated (
Theorem

4.21
). Finally, we deduce from this one of the two dualities of
[
15
, Theorem 0.1]

Corollary
4.28
.

The perfect pairing (
1.9
) induces a perfect cup-product pairing in
\mathbf{FLCA}

\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}})_{\wedge}\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{F})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},

where we set

\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}})_{\wedge}\coloneqq\mathrm{Ker}((\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge}\rightarrow(\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge}).

In the Appendix
A
we show how to define condensed pairings for abelian varieties and
1
-motives, and we prove their compatibility with the classical Poincaré pairings. These pairings are needed for the statement of the duality theorems of
Section

4
.

After presenting some generalities on cup-product pairings in a topos and their compatibility with respect to morphisms of topoi, we recall the notion of biextension in a topos, which is introduced in
[
14
, VII]
and is related to pairings. This relationship can be summarised as follows. Let
\mathscr{T}
be a topos and let
A,B,G
be abelian objects of
\mathscr{T}
. The set
\mathrm{Biex}^{1}(A,B;G)
of biextensions of
(A,B)
by
G
modulo isomorphism is endowed with an abelian group structure. Moreover,
[
14
, VII, Corollary 3.6.5]
gives a canonical isomorphism of abelian groups

\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(A,B;G)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T})}(A\otimes^{L}B,G[1]).

(1.10)

In Section
A.3
we analyse biextensions of commutative group schemes over a
p
-adic field
F
. In particular, we suppose that
A,B
are commutative
F
-group schemes locally of finite type, and that
G
is an affine algebraic group over
F
. First, we show that if
W
is a biextension of
(A,B)
by
G
, then the discrete
W_{F}
-set
W(\overline{L})
is a biextension of
(A(\overline{L}),B(\overline{L}))
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})
in the topos of discrete
W_{F}
-sets. Afterwards, we use the condensed Weil-étale realisation to produce condensed biextensions in
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
under the assumption that
G=\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. Indeed, we prove the following

Theorem
A.11
.

Let
A,B
be commutative group schemes locally of finite type over
F
, and let
W
be a biextension of
(A,B)
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. Then its condensed Weil-étale realisation
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a biextension of
(A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},B(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
.

We also show the compatibility with the classical cup-product pairing in the following

Proposition
A.14
.

Let
A,B
be commutative group schemes locally of finite type over
F
, and let
W
be a biextension of
(A,B)
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. Then
W
, its Weil-étale realisation
W(\overline{L})
and its condensed Weil-étale realisation
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
induce compatible cup-product pairings. In other words, the following diagram has commutative squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(G_{F},A(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(G_{F},B(\overline{F}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{F}))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(W_{F},A(\overline{L}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(W_{F},B(\overline{L}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(W_{F},\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L}))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},B(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W(\overline{L})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

We apply this discussion to the Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of an abelian variety
A
and its dual
A^{*}
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, obtaining a biextension
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
of
(A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
. This condensed biextension induces the condensed Poincaré pairing (
1.6
)

\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\otimes^{L}A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[1],

which is needed for
Theorem

4.19
.

Finally, in Section
A.4
, we recall the generalisation of the concept of biextension to two-term complexes, as explained in
[
9
]
. This allows to define biextensions of
1
-motives. As a Corollary to
Theorem

A.11
, we prove its analogue for
1
-motives

Corollary
A.21
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{1}=[Y_{1}\to E_{1}]
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=[Y_{2}\to E_{2}]
be two
1
-motives over
F
, and let
W
be a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. Then
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
.

We conclude by applying this result to the Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}
of a
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}
and its Cartier dual
\mathcal{M}^{*}
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, defined in
[
9
, 10.2.11]
. We obtain the condensed Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
by
(\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
and the condensed Poincaré pairing (
1.8
)

\psi_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[1],

which is needed for
Theorem

4.21
. Finally, we show the compatibility of this condensed pairing with the condensed weight filtration in
Lemma

A.30
.

In the Appendix
B
, written by Takashi Suzuki, the relation between Theorem
F
and
Theorem

4.20
is treated.

1.3
Preliminaries and notation

Most of these coincide with the conventions of
[
3
]
.

1.3.1
Set-theoretical conventions

A category
C
is small if both
\mathrm{Ob}(C)
and
\mathrm{Mor}(C)
are sets. A category is essentially small if it is equivalent to a small category. We fix an uncountable strong limit cardinal
\kappa>\aleph_{1}
. We say that a set
S
is
\kappa
-small if we have
|S|<\kappa
.

1.3.2
Topos theory

We make use of topos theory, which main reference is
[
2
]
. If
T
is a topos and
X
is an object of
T
, we denote by
T/X
the induced topos
[
2
, IV.5.1]
. We call the canonical morphism of topoi
j_{X}:T/X\rightarrow T
localisation morphism. If
G
is a group object in
T
, we denote by
B_{G}(T)
the classifyig topos of
G
(see
[
2
, IV.2.4]
), which is the category of objects of
T
with an action of
G
. By functoriality of this construction, we have a canonical morphism of topoi

f_{G}:B_{G}(T)\rightarrow B_{*}(T)=T.

For example, if
G
is a group,
B_{G}(\mathbf{Set})
is the category of
G
-sets. Then the morphism
f_{G}
is such that the functor
f_{G,*}
is the fixed points functors

f_{G,*}:B_{G}(\mathbf{Set})\rightarrow\mathbf{Set},\quad X\mapsto X^{G}.

Let
\hat{G}=(G_{i})_{i\in I}
be a pro-object of the category
\mathbf{Grp}(T)
. As in
[
3
, 2.3]
, we define the classifying topos of
\hat{G}
as

B_{\hat{G}}(T)\coloneqq\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
i\in I\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,B_{G_{i}}(T).

We have a canonical morphism of topoi

f_{\hat{G}}:B_{\hat{G}}(T)\rightarrow T.

For example if
\hat{G}
is the Galois group of a field
F
seen as a pro-object of the topos
T=\mathbf{Set}
, then
B_{\hat{G}}(\mathbf{Set})
is the category of discrete Galois modules. In this case, the morphism
f_{\hat{G}}
is such that
f_{\hat{G},*}
is the fixed points functor.

Whenever the topos
T
is the topos of (
\kappa
)-condensed sets, we denote
B_{G}(T)
and
B_{\hat{G}}(T)
simply by
B_{G}
and
B_{\hat{G}}
.

1.3.3
\infty
-derived categories

We use the theory of stable
\infty
-categories, developed in
[
20
]
. If
A
is an abelian category, we denote by
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(A)
its bounded derived
\infty
-category. It is a stable
\infty
-category whose homotopy category is the classical bounded derived category
D^{\mathrm{b}}(A)
. If
T
is a topos, we denote by
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(T)
the bounded derived
\infty
-category
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(T))
.

If
QA
is a quasi-abelian category in the sense of
[
26
]
, we denote by
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(QA)
its bounded derived
\infty
-category in the sense of
[
10
, 2.1]
. It is a stable
\infty
-category whose homotopy category is the bounded derived category
D^{\mathrm{b}}(QA)
in the sense of
[
26
]
.

1.3.4
Locally compact abelian groups and Pontryagin duality

We denote by
\mathbf{LCA}
the quasi-abelian category of
\kappa
-small locally compact abelian groups. Unless stated otherwise, by locally compact we mean
\kappa
-small locally compact. The category
\mathbf{FLCA}
of locally compact abelian groups of finite ranks (see
[
16
]
) is a full subcategory of
\mathbf{LCA}
. Indeed, all locally compact abelian groups of finite ranks are
\kappa
-small. For a locally compact abelian group
A
, we denote by
A^{\vee}
its Pontryagin dual, i.e. the locally compact abelian group
\Hom_{\mathbf{LCA}}(A,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
with the compact-open topology. The Pontryagin duality induces equivalences of categories
\mathbf{LCA}^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\mathbf{LCA}
and
\mathbf{FLCA}^{\mathrm{op}}\cong\mathbf{FLCA}
.

Both
\mathbf{LCA}
and
\mathbf{FLCA}
are quasi-abelian categories, and we can consider their bounded derived
\infty
-categories
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
and
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. As showed in
[
10
, 2.1]
, the functor
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})\to\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
is an exact and fully faithful functor of stable
\infty
-categories. It is also
t
-exact. Moreover, Pontryagin duality gives an equivalence

\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA}),\quad X\mapsto X^{\vee}\coloneqq R\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{LCA}}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

and similarly for
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
.

1.3.5
Condensed Mathematics

We denote by
\mathscr{C}
the topos of
\kappa
-condensed sets as defined in
[
7
]
. This is the category of sheaves of sets on the site of
\kappa
-small profinite sets, with the topology where coverings are given by finitely jointly surjective maps. We can also restrict the defining site to
\kappa
-small extremally disconnected topological spaces. If
\mathbf{Top}^{cg}
denotes the category of
\kappa
-compactly generated topological spaces (i.e. such that the continuity of functions can be checked on
\kappa
-small compact Hausdorff subspaces), we have a fully faithful functor

\underline{(-)}:\mathbf{Top}^{cg}\rightarrow\mathscr{C},\quad X\mapsto\underline{X}\coloneqq\Cont(-,X).

Thus
\mathscr{C}
is a topos which is able to see topological phenomena.
Unless stated otherwise, condensed (resp. compactly generated, resp. profinite, resp. extremally disconnected) means
\kappa
-condensed (resp.
\kappa
-compactly generated, resp.
\kappa
-small profinite, resp.
\kappa
-small extremally disconnected).

The functor
\underline{(-)}
respects all limits. Consequently, the category
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
contains
\mathbf{LCA}
as full subcategory. Moreover, the internal Hom can be equivalently computed in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
or in
\mathbf{LCA}
(see
[
7
, Lecture IV]
). As it is showed in
[
3
, 4.1]
, we have a functor
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})\rightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
which is an exact and fully faithful functor of stable
\infty
-categories. Moreover, the computation of
R\underline{\Hom}(-,-)
can equivalently be done in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
or
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
. In this sense, the Pontryagin duality can be seen as an antiequivalence of a stable
\infty
-subcategory of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
.

If
A
is a condensed abelian group and
m\in\mathbbm{N}
, we denote by
\prescript{}{m}{A}
and
A/m
the kernel and the cokernel of the multiplication by
m
respectively. Moreover, we denote by
TA
the Tate module of
A
, i.e. the cofiltered limit of
\prescript{}{m}{A}
along the morphisms
\cdot d:\prescript{}{d\cdot m}{A}\rightarrow\prescript{}{m}{A}
. The non-derived profinite completion of
A
is denoted by
A^{\wedge}
, while the derived completion of any complex of abelian groups
C
is denoted by
C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}
.

1.3.6
The Weil group of a
p
-adic field

Unless stated otherwise,
F
denotes a fixed
p
-adic field, with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{F}
and residue field
k
. The units of the ring of integers are denoted by
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}
. We fix separable closures of
F
and
k
and we call them
\overline{F}
and
\overline{k}
respectively. We denote by
F^{\mathrm{un}}
the maximal unramified extension of
F
, and by
L
the completion of
F^{\mathrm{un}}
. We fix a separable closure
\overline{L}
of
L
containing
\overline{F}
. We denote by
G_{k}
,
G_{F}
and by
I_{F}
the absolute Galois groups of
k
,
F
and
F^{\mathrm{un}}
respectively. By Krasner’s Lemma we also have
I_{F}=\Gal(\overline{L}/L)
. We have a natural isomorphism of topological groups
G_{k}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}
sending the Frobenius automorphism to
1
.

Definition 1.1
.

The Weil groups of
k
and
F
are defined as follows

(i)

The Weil group of
k
, denoted
W_{k}
, is defined by the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}

(ii)

The Weil group of
F
is defined as
W_{F}\coloneqq G_{F}\times_{G_{k}}W_{k}
.

We have an exact sequence of topological groups

1\rightarrow I_{F}\rightarrow W_{F}\rightarrow W_{k}\rightarrow 1.

We see that
W_{F}
is a prodiscrete topological group, inverse limit of discrete groups
W_{F}/U
, with
U
varying among the open normal subgroups of
I_{F}
. If
K
is a finite extension of
L
with absolute Galois group
U
, the discrete groups
W_{F}/U
acts on
K
and the fixed points are
F
. We get a continuous action of
W_{F}
on
\overline{L}
with fixed points
F
.

1.3.7
The condensed cohomology of the Weil group

We use the results and the notation of
[
3
, §3]
. Thanks to the functor
\underline{(-)}:\mathbf{Top}^{cg}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}
, we see the prodiscrete group
W_{F}
as a pro-object of the category
\mathbf{Grp}(\mathscr{C})
of condensed groups, and we denote it
\hat{W}_{F}
. We consider its classifying topos
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
, i.e. the category of condensed sets with an action of the pro-condensed group
\hat{W}_{F}
. We get a functor

f_{\hat{W}_{F},*}:\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}),

whose derived functors give the cohomology theory
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},-)
. We do the same construction at the derived level, obtaining a functor

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},-):\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})\rightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C}).

This is the cohomology we use in this paper.

1.3.8
Group schemes and Cartier duality

The coefficients of
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},-)
come from algebraic geometry. We denote by
\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
the category of schemes which are locally of finite type over
F
, with morphisms of schemes as morphisms. We endow
\mathbf{Sch}^{lft}_{F}
with the fppf-topology, resp. the étale topology, and we call
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}}
, resp.
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}
, the corresponding topos. We denote the internal Hom’s
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})}(-,-)
and
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}})}(-,-)
simply by
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(-,-)
and
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}(-,-)
, and similarly for
\underline{\Ext}(-,-)
.

The category
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F})
of commutative group schemes which are locally of finite type over
F
is a full subcategory of both
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})
and
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}})
. If
G
is either finite or a torus, the object
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(G,\mathbbm{G}_{m})
is representable by a certain
G^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Ab}(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F})
. Such
G^{\prime}
is called
Cartier dual
of
G
, and we also have
G=\underline{\Hom}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(G^{\prime},\mathbbm{G}_{m})
. If
G
is finite, then so is
G^{\prime}
. If
G
is a torus (étale-locally isomorphic to
\mathbbm{G}_{m}^{r})
, then
G^{\prime}
is étale-locally isomorphic to
\mathbbm{Z}^{r}
. We obtain a natural biadditive map

G\times G^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}

called
Cartier pairing
. If
A
is an abelian variety, the sheaf
\underline{\Ext}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(A,\mathbbm{G}_{m})
is represented by the dual abelian variety
A^{*}
. Moreover, the Poincaré biextension gives a pairing

A\otimes^{L}A^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})
. We discuss this pairing more in detail in Appendix
A
.

1.4

This work was carried out during my PhD at the Université de Bordeaux under the supervision of Baptiste Morin, whose support was crucial and to whom I am deeply grateful. I would like to thank Qing Liu for answering to my questions on the topology of rational points of abelian varieties. I am thankful to Adrien Morin for the exchange we had on biextensions. I am grateful to Clark Barwick and Tamás Szamuely for their valuable comments.

2
Condensed Weil-étale realisation

In
[
8
]
, Conrad shows how the topology of
F
functorially defines a topology on the
F
-points of those schemes which are locally of finite type over
F
. We use this technique to associate to every commutative group scheme locally of finite type over
F
, resp. every
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}
over
F
, a
condensed Weil-étale realisation
. This object is a condensed abelian group, resp. a complex of abelian groups, with an action of
\hat{W}_{F}
.

2.1
Realisation of algebraic groups

Let
K
be a topological field. By
[
8
, Proposition 3.1]
, there is a functor

\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{K}\rightarrow\mathbf{Top},\qquad X\mapsto X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}

(2.1)

such that:

i)

if
X\hookrightarrow Y
is a closed (resp. open) immersion of schemes, then
X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}\hookrightarrow Y(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
is a topological embedding (resp. open embedding).

ii)

we have
(X\times_{Z}Y)(K)^{\mathrm{top}}=X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}\times_{Z(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}Y(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
.

iii)

we have
X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}=K
with its topology when
X=\mathbbm{A}^{1}_{K}
.

iv)

(
[
8
, Example 2.2]
) Let
K^{\prime}/K
be a finite extension. Let
X
be a locally finite type
K
-scheme and let
X^{\prime}\coloneqq X\times_{K}K^{\prime}
. Then
X^{\prime}
is locally of finite type over
K^{\prime}
, and the natural map
X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}\to X(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}=X^{\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
is a topological closed embedding. Moreover, if
K_{1}\rightarrow K_{2}
is a continuous map of topological rings, and
X
and
X^{\prime}
are as above, the induced map
X(K_{1})^{\mathrm{top}}\to X(K_{2})^{\mathrm{top}}=X^{\prime}(K_{2})^{\mathrm{top}}
is continuous.

We apply this construction to define a condensed étale realisation of schemes which are locally of finite type over a topological field
K
which is complete with respect to a non-trivial norm
\lVert-\rVert_{K}
. Let
\overline{K}
be a separable closure of
K
. If
K^{\prime}/K
is a finite field extension contained in
\overline{K}
, then
\lVert-\rVert_{K}
uniquely extends on
K^{\prime}
, inducing a topology on
K^{\prime}
. We have
X_{K^{\prime}}\in\mathbf{Sch}_{K^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{lft}}
and we can define
X(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
as in (
2.1
). By iv), we get a continuous action of
\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)
on
X(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
. This leads to the following

Definition 2.1
.

Let
K
be a topological field which is complete with respect to a non-trivial norm. Let
X
be a scheme which is locally of finite type over
K
. We define the
condensed étale realisation
of
X
as

X(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\coloneqq\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
K^{\prime}/K\\
\text{ finite ext.}\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\underline{X(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}},

which can be naturally seen as an object of
B_{\hat{G}_{K}}
.

The condensed étale realisation defines a functor

-(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}:\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{K}\rightarrow B_{\hat{G}_{K}}.

which commutes with fiber products. This follows from the property ii) of Conrad’s functor, and from the fact that filtered colimits commute with fiber products. Thus we get a functor

-(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}:\mathbf{Ab}(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{K})\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{G}_{K}}),\qquad A\mapsto A(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}.

Example 2.2
.

If
A=\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, then we have
A(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}=\overline{K}^{\times}\coloneqq\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
K^{\prime}/K\\
\text{ finite ext}\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\underline{(K^{\prime})^{\times}}
.

Remark 2.3
.

If the norm is archimedean, then
(K,\lVert-\rVert_{K})
is isomorphic to either
\mathbbm{R}
or
\mathbbm{C}
with their usual norms. In both cases, the condensed étale realisation of any
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{K}
is
\underline{X(\mathbbm{C})^{\mathrm{top}}}\in B_{\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbbm{C}/K)}
, which is represented by a locally compact
\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbbm{C}/K)
-module (see
[
8
, Remark 3.2]
).

Let
F
be a
p
-adic field. We follow the same approach to define the condensed Weil-étale realisation of
F
-schemes locally of finite type. Let
L
be the completion of the maximal unramified extension
F^{\mathrm{un}}
. We fix an algebraic closure
\overline{L}
of
L
. For every field extension
K/L
contained in
\overline{L}
, the discrete valuation on
L
extends uniquely to
K
, defining a topology on it. Moreover, if we set
U_{K}\coloneqq\Gal(\overline{L}/K)
, the discrete group
W_{F}/U_{K}
acts continuously on
K
. We have the following

Definition 2.4
.

Let
X
be a scheme which is locally of finite type over
F
. We define the
condensed Weil-étale realisation
of
X
as

X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\coloneqq\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
K/L\\
\text{ finite ext.}\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\underline{X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}},

which can be naturally seen as an object of
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
.

We get the
condensed Weil-étale realisation
functor

-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}:\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}\rightarrow B_{\hat{W}_{F}}.

which commutes with fiber products and induces a functor

-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}:\mathbf{Ab}(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F})\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}}).

For every
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
, the pullback of
X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
via the morphism of topoi
B_{\hat{I}}\rightarrow B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
is the condensed étale realisation of
X_{L}
, i.e.
X_{L}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}
.

Example 2.5
.

We set
\mu_{n}\coloneqq\mathrm{Spec}(F[T]/(T^{n}-1))
. Then
\mu_{n}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\in\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
is represented by the finite abelian group
\mu_{n}(\overline{L})
of
n
th roots of unity in
\overline{L}
with a continuous action of
W_{F}
, which factors through a finite quotient.

Example 2.6
.

Let
M
be an abelian group, and let
M_{F}=\coprod_{m\in M}\mathrm{Spec}(F)
be the constant group scheme over
F
associated to
M
. By property i) of Conrad’s functor,
M(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
is discrete for all
K
finite extension of
L
. Thus we have

M_{F}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=M

with the discrete topology and the trivial action of
\hat{W}_{F}
.

Example 2.7
.

Let
G
be a commutative group scheme over
F
which is locally constant for the étale topology, isomorphic to
M_{F^{\prime}}
over a finite extension
F^{\prime}
of
F
. We have

G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=\underline{G(F^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}}=M

with the discrete topology and an action of
\hat{W}_{F}
which factors through
\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)
.

Example 2.8
.

Let
T
be a torus over
F
and let
Y^{*}
be its Cartier dual. Let
X^{*}(T)\coloneqq Y^{*}(\overline{F})
denote the group of characters of
T_{\overline{F}}
. It is a free finitely generated abelian group isomorphic to
\mathbbm{Z}^{r}
with a continuous action of
G_{F}
which factors through
\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)
. By
Example

2.7
, we have

Y^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=X^{*}(T)\in\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}}),

with the discrete topology and an action of
\hat{W}_{F}
which factors through the finite quotient
\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)
. The Cartier pairing

\gamma_{T}:T\times\mathcal{X}(T)\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}.

induces a bilinear map in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})

T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\times Y^{*}\rightarrow\overline{L}^{\times},

and consequently a morphism

T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}_{B_{\hat{W}_{F}}}(X^{*}(T),\overline{L}^{\times}).

(2.2)

We have the following

Proposition 2.9
.

Let
T
be a torus over
F
and let
X^{*}(T)
be the group of characters of
T_{\overline{F}}
. Then (
2.2
) is an isomorphism in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
.

Proof.

We have

T_{F^{\prime}}=(\mathbbm{G}_{m}^{r})_{F^{\prime}},\qquad Y^{*}_{F^{\prime}}=\mathbbm{Z}^{r}_{F^{\prime}}

for some
r\in\mathbbm{N}
and for some finite extension
F^{\prime}
of
F
. Let
K^{\prime}
be a finite extension of
L
containing
F^{\prime}
. By definition of
-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
we have

T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
K/K^{\prime}\\
\text{fin.\, ext.}\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\underline{T(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}.

Moreover, by
[
3
, Corollary 2.58, (i)]
we have

\underline{\Hom}_{B_{\hat{W}_{F}}}(X^{*}(T),\overline{L}^{\times})=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
K/K^{\prime}\\
\text{fin.\, ext.}\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\underline{\Hom}_{B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}}(X^{*}(T),\underline{K}^{\times}).

The morphism (
2.2
) is induced by morphisms

\varphi_{K}:\underline{T(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}_{B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}}(X^{*}(T),\underline{K}^{\times}).

It is enough to show that
\varphi_{K}
is an isomorphism in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{W_{F}/U_{K}})
for all
K
finite extension of
K^{\prime}
. Since the morphism of topoi
j:\mathscr{C}=B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}/EW_{F}/U_{K}\rightarrow B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}
is a localisation morphism, its pullback is conservative. Hence it is enough to show that the induced morphism

j^{*}\varphi_{K}:j^{*}\underline{T(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(j^{*}X^{*}(T),j^{*}\underline{K}^{\times})

is an isomorphism. In other words, we forget the action of the Weil group. Since we have
T_{F^{\prime}}=(\mathbbm{G}^{r}_{m})_{F^{\prime}}
and
Y^{*}_{F^{\prime}}=\mathbbm{Z}^{r}_{F^{\prime}}
, the morphism
j^{*}\varphi_{K}
is

j^{*}\varphi_{K}:\underline{\mathbbm{G}_{m}^{r}(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathbbm{Z}^{r},\underline{\mathbbm{G}_{m}(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}),

which is an isomorphism in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
. This concludes the proof.
∎

2.2
Exactness of realisations

The goal of this section is to study the exactness of condensed étale realisation and condensed Weil-étale realisation functors.

Theorem 2.10
.

Let
K
be a complete field with respect to a non-trivial norm. Let

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow 0

be an exact sequence of smooth commutative algebraic groups over
K
.

•

If the norm is archimidean, the corresponding sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{G}_{K}})

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow A(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow 0

is exact.

•

If the norm is non-archimedean, the corresponding sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{G}_{K}})

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow A(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow 0

is exact if the topological abelian groups
A(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}},A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
are prodiscrete for every finite extension
K^{\prime}
of
K
.

Proof.

Since the functor

-(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}:\mathbf{Ab}(\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{K})\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{G}_{K}})

respects fiber products, the only thing to show is that the morphism
A(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}
is an epimorphism in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{G}_{K}})
.

Let
K^{\prime}/K
be a finite field extension. Since the morphism
A_{K^{\prime}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}_{K^{\prime}}
is smooth, the induced map
f_{K^{\prime}}:A(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}\to A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
is open (see the discussion before
[
8
, Theorem 4.5]
).

If the norm is archimedean, we apply this observation to
K^{\prime}=\overline{K}=\mathbbm{C}
. The map
f_{K^{\prime}}
is a strict epimorphism between locally compact abelian groups. Consequently, it induces an epimorphism on the associated condensed abelian groups (see
[
7
, Lecture IV]
).

We now suppose that the norm is non-archimedean and that
A(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}},A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
are prodiscrete for all finite extension
K^{\prime}/K
. Let
I_{K^{\prime}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Im}(f_{K^{\prime}})
. Since
f_{K^{\prime}}
is open, the subgroup
I_{K^{\prime}}
is closed and open in
A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
. Consequently, the topological abelian group
A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}/I_{K^{\prime}}
is discrete, and we have an exact sequence of condensed abelian groups

0\rightarrow\underline{I_{K^{\prime}}}\rightarrow\underline{A(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})/I_{K^{\prime}}}\rightarrow 0.

Since
I_{K^{\prime}}
is a closed subgroup of
A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
and since the latter is prodiscrete, then so is
I_{K^{\prime}}
. By
[
1
, Lemma 3.1]
, the map
A(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}\rightarrow I_{K^{\prime}}
induces an epimorphism on the associated condensed abelian groups. We obtain an exact sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})

0\rightarrow\underline{A^{\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{A(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{I_{K^{\prime}}}\rightarrow 0,

which identifies
\underline{I_{K^{\prime}}}
with
\mathrm{Coim}(\underline{f_{K^{\prime}}})=\mathrm{Im}(\underline{f_{K^{\prime}}})
. Thus we have

\mathrm{Coker}(\underline{f_{K^{\prime}}})=\underline{A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}}/\underline{I_{K^{\prime}}},

which is discrete by the previous discussion. This holds in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
but also in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)})
, being the left adjoint of the localisation morphism
j_{E\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)}:\mathscr{C}\rightarrow B_{\Gal(K^{\prime}/K)}
conservative. As a consequence, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{Coker}(A(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{K})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}})&=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
K^{\prime}/K\\
\text{fin.\ ext.}\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathrm{Coker}(\underline{A(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}})=\\
&=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
K^{\prime}/K\\
\text{fin.\ ext.}\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\underline{A^{\prime\prime}(K^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}/I_{K^{\prime}}},\end{split}

which is discrete and coincides with the cokernel of
A(\overline{K})\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{K})
, which is
0
. This concludes the proof.
∎

We deduce an analogous result for the condensed Weil-étale realisation.

Corollary 2.11
.

Let
F
be a
p
-adic field and let
L
be the completion of its maximal unramified extension
F^{\mathrm{un}}
. Let

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow 0

be an exact sequence of commutative algebraic groups over
F
. Suppose that for every finite field extension
K
of
L
the topological abelian groups
A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
and
A^{\prime\prime}(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
are prodiscrete. Then the corresponding sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow 0.

is exact.

Remark 2.12
.

We don’t need the smoothness hypothesis anymore, since every algebraic group over a field of characteristic
0
is smooth (see
[
22
, Corollary 8.38]
).

Proof.

By
Theorem

2.10
we have an exact sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{I}})

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow 0.

Let us call
j_{EW_{k}}
the localisation morphism

B_{\hat{W}_{F}}/EW_{k}=B_{\hat{I}}\rightarrow B_{\hat{W}_{F}}.

We have
j_{EW_{K}}^{*}\circ-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}}
. Conservativity of
j_{EW_{K}}^{*}
implies that the sequence

0\rightarrow A^{\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow A^{\prime\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow 0

is exact in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
.
∎

The hypothesis of prodiscreteness of
A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
and
A^{\prime\prime}(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
seems hard to check apriori, but in fact it is often satisfied for algebraic groups over
p
-adic fields. I conjecture that if
F
is a
p
-adic field, this hypothesis holds for all algebraic groups. We will see that it is satisfied in all the cases we are interested in, i.e. when
A
is an abelian variety, a semiabelian variety or a torus over the
p
-adic field
F
.

2.3
Realisation of
1
-
motives

By
[
25
]
, a 1-motive
\mathcal{M}
over
F
is the following data:

•

A
F
-group scheme
Y
which is étale locally isomorphic to the constant group
\mathbbm{Z}^{r}
for some
r\in\mathbbm{N}
.

•

A semiabelian variety
E
over
F
, i.e. an extension of an
F
-abelian variety
A
by an
F
-torus
T
.

•

A morphism of
F
-group schemes
u:Y\rightarrow E
.

We denote by
\mathcal{M}
the complex of
F
-group schemes
[Y\overset{u}{\longrightarrow}E]
, where
Y
is in degree
-1
and
E
is in degree
0
. This can be seen as a complex of fppf-sheaves over
F
and thus it represents an object of the derived category
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})
. The
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}
is equipped with a 3-term weight filtration

0\hookrightarrow W_{-2}\mathcal{M}\hookrightarrow W_{-1}\mathcal{M}\hookrightarrow W_{0}\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M},\qquad W_{-2}\mathcal{M}=[0\rightarrow T],\,\,W_{-1}\mathcal{M}=[0\rightarrow E]

where we have

\mathcal{M}/W_{-1}\mathcal{M}=[Y\rightarrow 0],\qquad W_{-1}\mathcal{M}/W_{-2}\mathcal{M}=[0\rightarrow A].

Definition 2.13
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
be a 1-motive over
F
.
We define the
condensed Weil-étale realisation
of
\mathcal{M}
as the complex

\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\coloneqq[Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\overset{u(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}{\longrightarrow}E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}],

where
Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is in degree
-1
and
E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is in degree
0
.

The complex
\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
represents an object of the derived category
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
. The existence of a condensed weight filtration for
\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a consequence of
Corollary

2.11
.

Proposition 2.14
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
be a 1-motive over
F
. We have a filtration

0\hookrightarrow W_{-2}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\hookrightarrow W_{-1}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\hookrightarrow W_{0}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}

with
W_{-2}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\coloneqq[0\rightarrow T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}]
,
W_{-1}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\coloneqq[0\rightarrow E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}]
. Moreover, we have

\begin{split}gr^{0}\coloneqq W_{0}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}/W_{-1}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}&=[Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow 0],\\
gr^{-1}\coloneqq W_{-1}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}/W_{-2}\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}&=[0\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}],\\
\end{split}

Proof.

By definition, we have a fibre sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})

E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[0]\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[1].

This gives

gr^{0}=[Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\to 0].

Moreover, by
Corollary

2.11
we have a fibre sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})

T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[0]\rightarrow E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[0]\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[0].

This gives

gr^{-1}=[0\to A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}].

∎

Remark 2.15
.

To use
Corollary

2.11
, one should prove that
E(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
and
A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
are prodiscrete for every finite field extension
K
of
L
. This is done in
Proposition

3.3
and
Proposition

3.10
. We can say that the proof of
Proposition

2.14
will be complete only after the proof of such results. This does not cause any problem since we will not use this result before
Section

3.4
.

3
Condensed structures on cohomology groups

If
G
is an algebraic group over
F
, then
G(\overline{L})
is a discrete
W_{F}
-module, and its cohomology groups are given by

\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),G(\overline{L}))=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
K/L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)}(\mathbf{Set}),G(K)).

The condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is a topologised version of
\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),G(\overline{L}))
. Indeed we have the following

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
G
be an algebraic group over
F
. Then for all
q
the canonical map

\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),G(\overline{L}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})

induced by the morphism of topoi
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}\to B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})
is an isomorphism of abelian groups.

Proof.

For all
K/L
finite extension, we have a morphism of topoi

\alpha_{K}:B_{W_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)}\to B_{W_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)}(\mathbf{Set})

such that
\alpha_{K*}
is exact and induces an isomorphism of abelian groups

\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)}(\mathbf{Set}),G(K))\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)},\underline{G(K)^{\mathrm{top}}})

for all
q
(see
[
3
, Proposition 2.41]
). The result follows.
∎

3.1
Coefficients in tori

A direct consequence of
Proposition

2.9
is the following

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
T
be a torus over
F
. We have

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbbm{Z}^{l}\oplus\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}^{m}\oplus H_{0}&q=0,\\
\mathbbm{Z}^{n}\oplus H_{1}&q=1,\\
0&q\geq 2.\end{array}

for some
l,m,n\in\mathbbm{N}
and some finite abelian groups
H_{0},H_{1}
.

Proof.

The proof of the first part of the statement is similar to the one of
[
3
, Theorem 3.31]
. The action of
\hat{W}_{F}
on
X^{*}(T)
is induced by a finite quotient of
G_{F}
, say
G\coloneqq\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)
. By
Proposition

2.9
, we have a spectral sequence

E_{2}^{i,j}=\mathbbm{H}^{i}(B_{G},\mathbbm{H}^{j}(B_{\hat{W}_{F^{\prime}}},\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\overline{L}^{\times}))\implies\mathbbm{H}^{i+j}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}).

As a
\hat{W}_{F^{\prime}}
-module, we have
\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\overline{L}^{\times})\cong(\overline{L}^{\times})^{r}
, where
r
is the rank of
X^{*}(T)
.
By
[
3
, Proposition 3.12]
, we have
E_{2}^{i,j}=0
for all
j\geq 2
. In addition to that, we have

E_{2}^{i,0}=\mathbbm{H}^{i}(B_{G},\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\underline{(F^{\prime})^{\times}})),\qquad E_{2}^{i,1}=\mathbbm{H}^{i}(B_{G},M).

where
M
is a free finitely generated abelian group with an action of the finite group
G
. Consequently,
E_{2}^{i,1}
is finite for all
i\geq 1
.
Let us consider the exact sequence of
G
-modules

0\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\underline{\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}})\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\underline{(F^{\prime})^{\times}})\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow 0

induced by the valuation
(F^{\prime})^{\times}\to\mathbbm{Z}
. In the proof of
[
3
, Theorem 3.31]
, it is shown that the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{i}(B_{G},\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\underline{\mathcal{O}_{F^{\prime}}}))
is finite for all
i\geq 1
. Consequently, the long exact cohomology sequence implies that
E_{2}^{i,0}
is finite for all
i\geq 1
.

To summarise, we showed that
E_{2}^{i,j}
is finite for all
i\geq 1
for all
j
, and vanishes for all
j\geq 2
for all
i
. Consequently, we have exact sequences for all
q\geq 1

0\rightarrow E_{\infty}^{q-1,1}\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow E_{\infty}^{q,0}\rightarrow 0

For
q\geq 2
, both
E_{\infty}^{q-1,1}
and
E_{\infty}^{q,0}
are finite, hence so is
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
. This implies that the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is represented by its underlying abelian group seen as a discrete topological group. Consequently we have, for all
q\geq 2

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})(*)=\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),T(\overline{L}))=0.

For
q=1
, the condensed abelian group
E_{\infty}^{q-1,1}
is a subgroup of
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{G},M)
. Consequently, it is a free finitely generated abelian group, while
E_{\infty}^{q,0}
is finite. The fact that
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is a finitely generated abelian group follows.

We just need to determine the structure of
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\underline{T(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}
. Since
T(F)^{\mathrm{top}}
is isomorphic to a finite power of
F^{\times}
as a topological abelian group, the result follows.
∎

3.2
Coefficients in abelian varieties

Let
A/F
be an abelian variety. We determine the structure of
A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
and of
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
for all
q
. We have the following

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
K/L
be a finite extension. Then the topological abelian group
A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
is prodiscrete.

Before proving this result, we make a few comments. We observe that
A_{K}
is an abelian variety over
K
and we have
A_{K}(K)=A(K)
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be the ring of integers of
K
, and let us call
\mathcal{A}
the Néron model of
A_{K}
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. We have an isomorphism of abelian groups

\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K})\cong A(K).

(3.1)

Moreover, the ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}\cong\underset{\leftarrow}{\lim}\,\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n}
is a complete Noetherian local ring. Consequently, the morphism of abelian groups

\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K})\rightarrow\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n})

(3.2)

induced by
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n})
is an isomorphism of abelian groups by
[
11
, §6]
.

By composing (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), we get an isomorphism

A(K)\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n}).

To deduce the prodiscreteness of
A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
, we have to show that (
3.1
) and (
3.2
) are actually homeomorphism between topological abelian groups. This is expressed by the following

Lemma 3.4
.

The following hold true:

(1)

Let
R\to R^{\prime}
be a continuous map of local topological rings, and let
X
be an
R
-scheme locally of finite type. We set
X^{\prime}\coloneqq X\times_{R}R^{\prime}
. Then the map
X(R)^{\mathrm{top}}\to X^{\prime}(R^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}=X(R^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
is continuous. Moreover, if
R\to R^{\prime}
is an open embedding, then so is
X(R)^{\mathrm{top}}\to X(R^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}
.

(2)

Let
R
be a complete discrete valuation ring with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
. Let
X
be an
R
-scheme which is locally of finite type. The morphism

\beta:X(R)^{\mathrm{top}}\rightarrow\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,X(R/\mathfrak{m}^{n})^{\mathrm{top}}

is a homeomorphism.

Remark 3.5
.

Let
R
be a local ring and let
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be an open cover of an
R
-scheme
X
. Then the family
\{U_{i}(R)^{\mathrm{top}}\}_{i\in I}
is an open cover of
X(R)^{\mathrm{top}}
. Indeed, for all
i
the morphism
U_{i}(R)^{\mathrm{top}}\subset X(R)^{\mathrm{top}}
is open by the properties of Conrad’s topology. Moreover, we have
\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}(R)^{\mathrm{top}}\subset X(R)
. To see that this is an equality, we take
f:\mathrm{Spec}(R)\to X
and we show that it factors through
U_{i}
for some
i
. Let
s
be the closed point of
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
. We have
f(s)\in U_{i}
for some
i
. The subset
f^{-1}(U_{i})
is an open subset of
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
containing
s
. Let
t
be prime ideal of
\mathrm{Spec}(R)\setminus f^{-1}(U_{i})
. The closure of
t
is contained in
\mathrm{Spec}(R)\setminus f^{-1}(U_{i})
but contains
s
, which is a contradiction. Thus we have
f^{-1}(U_{i})=\mathrm{Spec}(R)
.

Proof.

For (1), we use
Remark

3.5
to reduce to the case where
X
is affine. Now the result follows from
[
8
, Example 2.2]
.

In the hypotheses of (2), the map
\beta
is a bijection by
[
11
, §6, Proposition 2]
. We just need to show that
\beta
is a local homeomorphism. Consequently, by
Remark

3.5
we can suppose that
X
is affine. We have
X=\mathrm{Spec}(R[t_{1},\dots,t_{k}]/I)
for some
k\in\mathbbm{N}
and some ideal
I
. Then
X(R)^{\mathrm{top}}
is the closed subset of
R^{k}
defined by
\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\in R^{k}\,|f(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})=0\,\forall f\in I\}
. Similarly,
X(R/\mathfrak{m}^{n})^{\mathrm{top}}
is the closed subset of
(R/\mathfrak{m}^{n})^{k}
given by
\{(\overline{x_{1}},\dots,\overline{x_{k}})\in(R/\mathfrak{m}^{n})^{k}\,|\overline{f}(\overline{x_{1}},\dots,\overline{x_{k}})=0\,\forall f\in I\}
. Thus the map

\beta:X(R)^{\mathrm{top}}\rightarrow\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,X(R/\mathfrak{m}^{n})^{\mathrm{top}}

is an homeomorphism, being the restriction of the homeomorphism
R^{k}\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,(R/\mathfrak{m}^{n})^{k}
to the closed subset
X(R)^{\mathrm{top}}
.
∎

We are now ready to prove the structure result for
A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
.

Proof of
Proposition

3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the Néron model of
A_{K}
over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. By
Lemma

3.4
we have isomorphisms of topological abelian groups

A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}\cong\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K})^{\mathrm{top}}\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n})^{\mathrm{top}}.

Since
\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n}
is a discrete topological ring for all
n
, then
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n})^{\mathrm{top}}
is a discrete topological abelian group for all
n
. The result follows.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

There is another way of seeing that
A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
is prodiscrete for every finite extension
K
of
L
. Indeed, by
[
21
, Theorem 7]
, if
k
is a complete ultrametric field and
A
is an abelian variety of dimension
d
defined over
k
, then the topological abelian group
