A(k)^{\mathrm{top}}
contains an
open
subgroup
A^{o}
which is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{k}^{d}
as a topological abelian group.

Lemma 3.7
.

The condensed abelian groups
\prescript{}{m}{\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
and
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})/m
are finite for all
m,q\in\mathbbm{N}
. In particular, if we denote by
A(\overline{L})^{\delta}
the abelian group
A(\overline{L})
with the discrete topology, then the map of condensed abelian groups

A(\overline{L})^{\delta}\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}

induces equivalences

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})^{\delta})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m

for all
m,q\in\mathbbm{N}
.

Proof.

By
Propositions

3.3
and
2.11
we have an exact sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})

0\rightarrow A_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\longrightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\overset{\cdot m}{\longrightarrow}A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow 0.

The associated Kummer sequence gives an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})/m\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\prescript{}{m}{\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}\rightarrow 0.

for all
q,m\in\mathbbm{N}
. Since
A_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is finite, then so is
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
[
3
, Proposition 3.26]
. The first part of the statement follows. The map

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})^{\delta})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m

induces an equivalence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab})
between the complexes of underlying abelian groups (see
[
3
, Remark 3.22]
and
Proposition

3.1
). Since the cohomology groups of both terms are finite, then the map is an equivalence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
.
∎

Lemma 3.8
.

For all
q\geq 1
the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is torsion, i.e. we have

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
m\end{subarray}}{\lim}\prescript{}{m}{\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}

Proof.

Since the functor which takes the sections at extremally disconnected topological spaces commutes with all limits and colimits, it is enough to show that
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})(S)
is torsion for all
S
extremally disconnected and for all
q\geq 1
. Moreover, we can reduce to finite extensions of
L
. We call
K
such an extension and we set
U\coloneqq\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{L}/K)
. For all
q
, we have an exact sequence

\begin{split}0\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\mathbbm{H}^{q-1}(B_{I/U},\underline{A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}))(S)\rightarrow&\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}/U},\underline{A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}})(S)\rightarrow\\
&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{W_{k}},\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{I/U},\underline{A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}))(S)\rightarrow 0.\end{split}

(3.3)

By
[
3
, Proposition 2.41]
we have for all
q
and for
i=0,1

\mathbbm{H}^{i}(B_{W_{k}},\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{I/U},\underline{A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}))(S)=\mathrm{H}^{i}(B_{W_{k}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{I/U}(\mathbf{Set}),\Cont(S,A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}))),

which is torsion for all
q\geq 1
, since
I/U
is finite. Thus
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\underline{A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}})(S)
is torsion for all
q\geq 2
.

For
q=1
, it is enough to show that
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\underline{A(L)^{\mathrm{top}}})
is represented by a discrete torsion abelian group. The following argument is inspired by the proofs of
[
23
, Proposition 3.8]
and
[
18
, Lemma 5.4.3]
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be the Néron model for
A
over
\mathcal{O}_{F}
and let
\mathcal{A}_{k}
be its special fibre. Let us denote by
\mathcal{A}^{0}
the open subgroup scheme of
\mathcal{A}
whose generic fibre is
A
and whose special fibre is
\mathcal{A}_{k}^{id}
, the identity component of
\mathcal{A}_{k}
. By
[
6
, Theorem 7.2.1, Corollary 7.2.2]
the base change
\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L}
is the Néron model of
A_{L}=A\times_{F}L
over
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, whose special fibre is
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{k}}
. Moreover, the base change
\mathcal{A}^{0}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L}
is the open subgroup scheme of
\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L}
whose generic fibre is
A_{L}
and whose special fibre is
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{k}}^{id}
. For this last point, we are using the fact that the identity component of an algebraic group commutes with any extension of the base field (see
[
22
, Proposition 1.34]
). Then the topological abelian group
\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}=(\mathcal{A}^{0}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L})(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}
is an open subgroup of
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}=(\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L})(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}
. Since
\mathcal{A}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{L}
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, which is a complete Hausdorff local ring, Hensel’s lemma implies that the reduction map
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{k}(\overline{k})
is surjective. It follows that the open subgroup
\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}
is of finite index in
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}
and the quotient is isomorphic to
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\overline{k}})
, the component group of
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{k}}
. Consequently, we have an exact sequence of condensed
W_{k}
-modules

0\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\overline{k}}})\rightarrow 0,

which induces an exact sequence of condensed abelian groups

\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\underline{\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\underline{\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\underline{\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\overline{k}}}))\rightarrow 0.

(3.4)

We show the vanishing of
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\underline{\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}})
following
[
12
, Proof of Proposition 3]
. By
Lemma

3.4
we have an isomorphism of topological abelian groups

\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathfrak{p}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}},

compatible with the action of
W_{k}
. In particular, the continuous homomorphism
1-\varphi:\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}
is the projective limit of the continuous morphisms

(1-\varphi)_{n}:\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathfrak{p}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\mathfrak{p}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}

which are surjective for all
n
(see
[
12
, Proof of Proposition 3]
). By discreteness, we conclude that
\underline{(1-\varphi)_{n}}
is an epimorphism for all
n
, and since
B_{W_{k}}
is replete,
\underline{1-\varphi}
is an epimorphism of condensed
W_{k}
-modules (see
[
5
, Proposition 3.1.8]
). Consequently, we have

\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\underline{\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}})=\mathrm{coker}(\underline{\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}}\overset{\underline{1-\varphi}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}})=0.

Therefore, the exact sequence (
3.4
) gives an isomorphism of finite abelian groups

\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\underline{\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}})=\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\underline{\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A}_{\overline{k}})}).

Since
\underline{\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{\mathrm{top}}}\cong\underline{A(L)^{\mathrm{top}}}
as condensed
W_{k}
-modules, the result follows.
∎

We are ready to prove the structure result for the cohomology groups of
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
with coefficients in
A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
.

Proposition 3.9
.

Let
A
be an abelian variety over
F
. We have

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\begin{array}[]{ll}\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}^{n}\oplus H_{0}&q=0,\\
\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p}^{m}\oplus H_{1}&q=1,\\
0&q\geq 2.\end{array}

for some
n,m\in\mathbbm{N}
and some finite abelian groups
H_{0},H_{1}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

3.8
we have

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
m\end{subarray}}{\lim}\prescript{}{m}{\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}

for all
q\geq 1
. By
Lemma

3.7
, this coincides with

\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
m\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\prescript{}{m}{\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})^{\delta})}=\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})^{\delta}).

Consequently, for all
q\geq 1
we have

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})^{\delta}).

Consequently
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
vanishes for all
q\geq 2
and it is of finite
\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
-type for
q=1
(see
[
18
, Lemma 5.4.1, Lemma 5.4.3]
).

To conclude, by
[
3
, Corollary 2.26, (1)]
we have

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\rightarrow\\
K/L\\
\text{fin.\, ext.}\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{W_{F}/U_{K}},\underline{A(K)^{\mathrm{top}}})=\underline{A(F)^{\mathrm{top}}},

which is a profinite abelian group isomorphic to the direct sum of a finite power of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
and a finite abelian group (see
Remark

3.6
).

∎

3.3
Coefficients in semiabelian varieties

Let
E
be a semiabelian variety over
F
. We have an exact sequence of commutative algebraic groups over
F

0\rightarrow T\rightarrow E\rightarrow A\rightarrow 0.

(3.5)

for some abelian variety
A
and some torus
T
. We use
Corollary

2.11
to determine the structure of
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
in terms of the one of
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
and
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
. The hypothesis of
Corollary

2.11
is satisfied by
A
thanks to
Proposition

3.3
. The same holds for
E
by the following

Proposition 3.10
.

Let
K/L
be a finite extension. Then the topological abelian group
E(K)^{\mathrm{top}}
is prodiscrete.

Proof.

Since
A_{K}
and
T_{K}
are smooth and connected, then so is
E_{K}
, by
[
22
, Proposition 8.1]
. We observe that
E_{K}
contains no subgroup of type
\mathbbm{G}_{a}
. By
[
6
, Ch X.2, Theorem 2]
,
E_{K}
admits a Néron lft-model over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, we call it
\mathcal{E}
.

Now we proceed as in
Proposition

3.3
. By Néron mapping property and by
[
11
, §6]
, we have isomorphisms of abelian groups

\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{O}_{K})\cong E(K).

(3.6)

\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{O}_{K})\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n}).

(3.7)

By
Lemma

3.4
we have an isomorphism of topological abelian groups

E(K)^{\mathrm{top}}\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\\
n\end{subarray}}{\lim}\,\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n})^{\mathrm{top}}.

The result follows from discreteness of
\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{p}_{K}^{n}
.
∎

Proposition 3.11
.

Let
E
be a semiabelian variety over
F
. We have:

•

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}
is locally compact of finite ranks, extension of an open subgroup of finite index of
\underline{A(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}
by
\underline{T(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}
.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is discrete of finite ranks, extension of
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by a quotient of
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=0
for all
q\neq 0,1
.

Proof.

By
Proposition

3.10
and
Corollary

2.11
, we have a fibre sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}).

By combining
Propositions

3.9
and
3.2
, we have
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=0
for all
q\geq 2
. Moreover, we have an exact sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})

\begin{split}0&\rightarrow\underline{T(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{A(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\\
&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow 0.\end{split}

Since we have
\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}(\underline{A(F)^{\mathrm{top}}},\mathbbm{Z})=0
, the map

\underline{A(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})

has finite image. Consequently, the image of
\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\to\underline{A(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}
is represented by an open subgroup of
A(F)^{\mathrm{top}}
of finite index. The result follows.
∎

3.4
Coefficients in 1-motives

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
be a 1-motive over
F
and let
\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
be its condensed Weil-étale realisation, which represents an object of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
. We determine the structure of
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
. We start with the following remark. We can’t expect
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
to be in the essential image of
\mathbf{LCA}\subset\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
in general. Indeed,
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
contains the cokernel of the morphism
\underline{u^{0}}:\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}
, which is not always in
\mathbf{LCA}
as the following example shows.

Example 3.12
.

Let us consider
F=\mathbbm{Q}_{p}
(
p\geq 3
), and let
\mathcal{M}
be the 1-motive
[\mathbbm{Z}\overset{u}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}]
such that
u^{0}:\mathbbm{Z}\to\mathbbm{Q}_{p}^{\times}
is the injection sending
1
to
1+p
. Then, under the isomorphism
\mathbbm{Q}_{p}^{\times}\cong\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\oplus\mathbbm{F}_{p}^{\times}\oplus\mathbbm{Z}
, the image of
\mathbbm{Z}
is a dense subset of
\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
. Consequently, the cokernel of the induced map

\underline{\mathbbm{Z}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbbm{Q}_{p}^{\times}}

contains the condensed abelian group
\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}/\underline{\mathbbm{Z}}
, which is not locally compact.

Condensed Mathematics is able to deal with non-Hausdorff situations. Indeed, we can isolate the non-Hausdorff term via the following

Definition 3.13
.

(from
Section

4.1
, (
4.3
))
Let
u^{0}:Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}\to E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}
be the continuous morphism of locally compact abelian groups of finite ranks induced by
u
. Let
\overline{u^{0}(Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}})}
be the closure of
u^{0}(Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}})
in
E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}
. We define

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}\coloneqq\mathrm{coker}(\,\underline{u^{0}(Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}})}\rightarrow\underline{(\overline{u^{0}(Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}})})}\,)

and

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}\coloneqq\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})/\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}.

In
Example

3.12
, we have
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}=\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}/\underline{\mathbbm{Z}}
. The structure of the cohomology groups of
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is given by the following

Theorem 3.14
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
be a 1-motive over
F
. Then we have
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. Moreover, we have

•

\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is a discrete free finitely generated abelian group.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is an extension of
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}
by
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}
.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}
is the cokernel of a dense injective morphism of locally compact abelian groups of finite ranks.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}
is a locally compact abelian group of finite ranks.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is discrete of finite ranks.

•

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=0
for all
q\neq 0,\pm 1
.

Proof.

We have a fibre sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})[1].

The result follows by taking the long exact cohomology sequence and by
Proposition

3.11
and
[
3
, Theorem 3.30]
.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}
is the maximal locally compact quotient of the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
. Indeed, for all
A\in\mathbf{LCA}
we have

\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}(\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}},A)=0.

Thus any map
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\to A
factors through
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L}))^{\mathrm{lca}}
.

Moreover, whenever the continuous map
u^{0}:Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}\to E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}
is closed, the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}
vanishes and
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
is locally compact of finite ranks.

Example 3.16
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[\mathbbm{Z}\overset{u}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}]
be the 1-motive of
Example

3.12
, defined over
F=\mathbbm{Q}_{p}
. The morphism
u(\overline{L}):\mathbbm{Z}\to\overline{L}^{\times}
sends
1
to
1+p
. We determine the structure of the cohomology groups
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
. We have a long exact cohomology sequence

\begin{split}0&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{Z}\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{Q}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\\
&\rightarrow\mathbbm{Z}\overset{\beta}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times})^{\vee}\rightarrow 0.\end{split}

Here the morphism
\alpha:\mathbbm{Z}\to\mathbbm{Q}_{p}^{\times}
is the injection
1\mapsto 1+p
and the morphism

\beta:\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathbbm{Z})=\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times})=\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},L^{\times})

is the zero morphism, since the valuation of
1+p
is
0
and the cohomology group
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},L^{\times})
identifies with
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{W_{k}},\mathbbm{Z})\cong\mathbbm{Z}
via the valuation
L^{\times}\to\mathbbm{Z}
. Consequently we have

\begin{split}\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=0,\qquad\qquad&\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}=\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}/\underline{\mathbbm{Z}},\\
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}=(\mathbbm{Z}\oplus\mathbbm{F}_{p}^{\times})\oplus\mathbbm{Z}\qquad&\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\in\Ext_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}((J)^{\vee},\mathbbm{Z}),\end{split}

where
J
is the kernel of
G_{F}^{ab}\to G_{k}
.

3.5
The
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
-twist

In the following we define a
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
-twist for the complex
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
, and more generally for any
M\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
such that we have
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M)\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
. This operation allows us to express the duality theorem for
1
-motives as a Pontryagin duality.

Definition 3.17
.

Let
M\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
such that we have
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M)\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
. We define the
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
-twist of
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M)
as

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M;\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\coloneqq R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M)\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{LCA}}\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},

where
\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{LCA}}
is the derived tensor product of the category
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
(see
[
16
, Remark 4.3, i)]
).

Remark 3.18
.

Let
A^{\bullet},B^{\bullet}
be two objects of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
in the essential image of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
. Then we have

(A^{\bullet}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}B^{\bullet})^{\vee}=R\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}(A^{\bullet},(B^{\bullet})^{\vee})=R\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{LCA}}(A^{\bullet},(B^{\bullet})^{\vee})=(A^{\bullet}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{LCA}}B^{\bullet})^{\vee}.

This shows that the Pontryagin dual of
T_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}\coloneqq A^{\bullet}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}B^{\bullet}
coincides with the Pontryagin dual of
T_{\mathbf{LCA}}\coloneqq A^{\bullet}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{LCA}}B^{\bullet}
. Since we have
T_{\mathbf{LCA}}\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
, this object is dualisable, i.e. we have
((T_{\mathbf{LCA}})^{\vee})^{\vee}=T_{\mathbf{LCA}}
. Whenever
T_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}
is dualisable as well, we can conclude that
T_{\mathbf{LCA}}=T_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}
. However, this fails in many cases, even not elaborated ones.

Example 3.19
.

Consider, for example,
A^{\bullet}=\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
and
B^{\bullet}=\mathbbm{R}
. Then we have

(\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}\mathbbm{R})^{\vee}=(\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{LCA}}\mathbbm{R})^{\vee}=0.

Consequently, we have
\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{LCA}}\mathbbm{R}=0
. However, the complex
\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}\mathbbm{R}
is far from being zero. For example, the underlying abelian group of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}\mathbbm{R})=\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbbm{Z}}\mathbbm{R}
is an uncountably dimensional flat
\mathbbm{R}
-module.

With coefficients in tori, abelian varieties or their Cartier duals, the effect of the
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
twist on cohomology groups can be described as follows:

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
G
be either an abelian variety, a torus or a
F
-group scheme étale locally isomorphic to
\mathbbm{Z}^{r}
for some
r\in\mathbbm{N}
. Then we have

(a)

\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathrm{toptors}}
;

(b)

For
q=0,1
, we have

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathbbm{Z}}\otimes\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\oplus\mathbbm{H}^{q+1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathrm{toptors}}.

(c)

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
for
q\neq 0,\pm 1
.

Remark 3.21
.

If
A\in\mathbf{LCA}
,
A_{\mathrm{toptors}}
denotes the topological torsion of
A
and
A_{\mathbbm{Z}}
denotes the discrete torsionfree quotient of
A
defined in
[
16
, Proposition 2.2]
. If
A=\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
with
G
as in the hypotheses of the Proposition,
A_{\mathbbm{Z}}
is a free finitely generated abelian group and the tensor product
A_{\mathbbm{Z}}\otimes\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
, present in (b), is equivalently
\otimes_{\mathbf{LCA}}
or
\otimes_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}
(they are canonically isomorphic in this case).

Proof.

We have a fiber sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

where the middle term is
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R})\coloneqq R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{LCA}}\mathbbm{R}
. We denote by
f^{q}
the induced morphism
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R})
. The long exact cohomology sequence gives an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathrm{Coker}(f^{q})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(f^{q+1})\rightarrow 0.

By definition, we have

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R})=\underline{\Hom}(\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{R}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}).

Consequently, the Pontryagin dual of
f^{q}
is given by

(f^{q})^{\vee}:\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{R})\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),

whose kernel and cokernel are
\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})
and
\underline{\Ext}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})
respectively. By the structure results on
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
(
Propositions

3.2
and
3.9
and
[
3
, Theorem 3.30]
) we have

\underline{\Ext}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})=\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathrm{toptors}},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

and

\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})=\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathbbm{Z}},\mathbbm{Z})

and both are locally compact abelian groups, hence dualisable for the Pontryagin duality. Consequently, we have

\mathrm{Coker}(f^{q})=\mathrm{Ker}((f^{q})^{\vee})^{\vee}=\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathbbm{Z}},\mathbbm{Z})^{\vee}=\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathbbm{Z}}\otimes\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}

and

\mathrm{Ker}(f^{q+1})=\mathrm{Coker}((f^{q+1})^{\vee})=\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathrm{toptors}}^{\vee\vee}=\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})_{\mathrm{toptors}},

which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.22
.

The fact that
\underline{\Ext}(\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})
is locally compact is a key point, and it follows from structure results on cohomology of abelian varieties, tori and their Cartier duals. This does not hold in general if
G
is a semiabelian variety.

Example 3.23
.

We look more precisely at the
F
-group schemes considered in
Proposition

3.20
.

1.

Let
T
be a torus.
Propositions

3.2
and
3.20
give

\displaystyle\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbbm{Z}^{l}\oplus\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}^{m}\oplus H_{0}&q=0\\
\mathbbm{Z}^{n}\oplus H_{1}&q=1\\
0&q\geq 2\end{array}

\displaystyle\implies\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{ll}\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}^{m}\oplus H_{0}&q=-1\\
(\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{l}\oplus H_{1}&q=0\\
(\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{n}&q=1\\
0&q\neq 0,\pm 1.\end{array}

2.

For
T=\mathbbm{G}_{m}
we have (following
[
3
, Proposition 3.12]
)

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}&q=-1\\
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}&q=0\\
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}&q=1\\
0&q\neq 0,\pm 1.\end{array}

3.

Let
A
be an abelian variety.
Propositions

3.9
and
3.20
give

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=\mathbbm{H}^{q-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=\begin{array}[]{ll}\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}^{a}\oplus H^{\prime}_{0}&q=-1\\
(\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p})^{b}\oplus H^{\prime}_{1}&q=0\\
0&q\neq-1,0.\end{array}

4.

Let
Y
be an
F
-scheme étale locally isomorphic to a free finitely generated abelian group.
Proposition

3.20
and
[
3
, Theorem 3.30]
give

\displaystyle\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbbm{Z}^{r}&q=0\\
\mathbbm{Z}^{s}\oplus H^{\prime\prime}_{1}&q=1\\
(\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p})^{t}\oplus H^{\prime\prime}_{2}&q=2\\
0&q\geq 3\end{array}

\displaystyle\implies\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

\displaystyle=\begin{array}[]{ll}(\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{r}\oplus H^{\prime\prime}_{1}&q=0\\
(\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{s}\oplus(\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p})^{t}\oplus H^{\prime\prime}_{2}&q=1\\
0&q\neq 0,1.\end{array}

5.

For
Y=\mathbbm{Z}
we have (following
[
3
, Lemma 3.28]
)

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathbbm{Z};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}&q=0\\
(W_{F}^{ab})^{\vee}&q=1\\
0&q\neq 0,1.\end{array}

4
Duality

In this section we prove duality result for the cohomology of
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
with coefficients in some objects coming from algebraic geometry. Our dualities are expressed as Pontryagin duality between complexes in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
.

4.1
Pontryagin duality in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})

Every statement in this section holds if we replace the category
\mathbf{FLCA}
with
\mathbf{LCA}
.

The Pontryagin duality in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
is given by the equivalence of categories

R\underline{\Hom}(-,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}):\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})\rightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA}).

Our duality results are expressed as perfect pairings in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})

C\otimes^{L}D\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

(4.1)

Such a perfect pairing identifies
C
with the Pontryagin dual of
D
and vice-versa. The goal of this section is to deduce from such a perfect pairing a similar duality on the cohomology groups of
C
and
D
.

Remark 4.1
.

As explained in
[
3
, 4.1]
, the functor
\underline{(-)}:\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})\to\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
allows us to see the first category as a full stable
\infty
-category of the second. Moreover, this functor is t-exact and induces an exact and fully faithful functor

\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}),\qquad[X^{-1}\rightarrow X^{0}]\,\mapsto\underline{X^{0}}/\underline{X^{-1}}.

These statements are precisely
[
3
, Lemma 4.2]
and
[
3
, Lemma 4.3]
.

Let us consider the perfect pairing (
4.1
). If
\mathrm{H}^{n}(D)
(and consequently
\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)
) is locally compact for all
n
, then the spectral sequence

E_{2}^{i,j}=\underline{\Ext}^{i}(\mathrm{H}^{-j}(D),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\implies\underline{\Ext}^{i+j}(D,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

degenerates. For all
n
, we get
\mathrm{H}^{n}(R\underline{\Hom}(D,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}))=(\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D))^{\vee}
, whence a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{FLCA}

\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)\otimes\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

This identifies
\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)
with the Pontryagin dual of
\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)
and vice-versa.

However, in general some cohomology groups of
C
and
D
may not be in
\mathbf{FLCA}
. In this case, we can’t deduce a perfect pairing between the cohomology groups so directly.

Example 4.2
.

We set

C\coloneqq[\mathbbm{Z}\overset{f}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{Z}_{p}],\quad D\coloneqq[\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\overset{f^{\vee}}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}]

where
f
is a continuous map,
\mathbbm{Z}
is in degree
-1
and
\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
is in degree
0
. Tautologically, we have a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))

C\otimes^{L}D\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}

i)

If
f
is the zero map, the cohomology groups of
C
and
D
are locally compact, and we obtain perfect pairings

\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\otimes\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},\qquad\mathbbm{Z}\otimes\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},\

which realise
\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)
and
\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C)
as the Pontryagin duals of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)
respectively.

ii)

If
f
is the
\cdot p
-multiplication
\cdot p:\mathbbm{Z}\to\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
, then it is injective and the image is a dense subset of
p\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
. Moreover,
f^{\vee}
has a dense image, and its kernel is given by the
p
-torsion of
\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
. In this case,
\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)
are not locally compact. Moreover,
\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)
is not the Pontryagin dual of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)
and
\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C)
is not the Pontryagin dual of
\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)
. Indeed, we have

\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C)=0,\quad\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)=\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}/\underline{p\mathbbm{Z}},\quad\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)=\mathbbm{Z}/p,\quad\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)=\underline{\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}}/\underline{\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}.

Something similar happens in the category
\mathbf{D}^{perf}(\mathbbm{Z})
, where instead of the Pontryagin duality we consider the
\mathbbm{Z}
-linear duality.

Example 4.3
.

We set

C\coloneqq[\mathbbm{Z}\oplus\mathbbm{Z}\overset{f}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{Z}],\quad D\coloneqq[\mathbbm{Z}\overset{g}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{Z}\oplus\mathbbm{Z}],

where
f
is a morphism of abelian groups and
g
is the
\mathbbm{Z}
-linear dual of
f
. Here
\mathbbm{Z}\oplus\mathbbm{Z}
is in degree
-1
for
C
and in degree
1
for
D
. Tautologically, we have a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{perf}(\mathbbm{Z})

C\otimes^{L}D\rightarrow\mathbbm{Z}.

i)

Let
f
be the zero map. Both cohomology groups of
C
and
D
are free, and we obtain
\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)
as the
\mathbbm{Z}
-linear dual of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)
and
\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)
as the
\mathbbm{Z}
-linear dual of
\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C)
.

ii)

Let
f
be the morphism
f(n,m)\coloneqq p\cdot m
(and thus let
g
be given by
g(n)\coloneqq(0,p\cdot n)
). Then we have

\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C)=\mathbbm{Z},\quad\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)=\mathbbm{Z}/p,\quad\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)=0,\quad\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)=\mathbbm{Z}\oplus\mathbbm{Z}/p.

The behaviour is different from the case i) but we still obtain a duality. Indeed, the maximal free quotient
\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)_{\mathbbm{Z}}
is the
\mathbbm{Z}
-linear dual of
\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C)
, while the torsion subgroup
\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)_{\mathrm{tors}}
is determined by
\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)
via
\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)_{\mathrm{tors}}=\Ext(\mathrm{H}^{0}(C),\mathbbm{Z})
.

In this example, we see how the duality can be recovered by separating, for any finitely generated abelian group, its torsion subgroup from its maximal free quotient. The goal of this section is to do something similar in the category
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. The role of the torsion subgroup is taken by the
non-Hausdorff
subgroup, and the role of the maximal free quotient is taken by the maximal
locally compact
quotient.

We define, for any
M\in\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
which is in the essential image of
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, a unique (up to isomorphism) exact sequence

0\rightarrow M^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow M\rightarrow M^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0,

(4.2)

and we prove the following

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
C,D\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
be two objects in the essential image of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})\to\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
. Suppose that we have a perfect pairing

C\otimes^{L}D\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

Then for all
n\in\mathbbm{N}
we have induced perfect pairings in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})

\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{lca}}\otimes\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}

and perfect pairings in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})

\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{nh}}\otimes^{L}\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D)^{\mathrm{nh}}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

The first pairing identifies
\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{lca}}
with
\underline{\Hom}(\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)^{\mathrm{lca}},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
, while the second one identifies
\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{nh}}
with
\underline{\Ext}(\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
and viceversa.

We go back to the second item of
Example

4.2
. The decomposition (
4.2
) for
\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)
becomes

0\rightarrow\underline{p\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}/\underline{p\mathbbm{Z}}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)\rightarrow\mathbbm{Z}/p\rightarrow 0.

We obtain the perfect pairings

\underline{p\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}/\underline{p\mathbbm{Z}}\otimes^{L}(\underline{\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}})/(\underline{\mathbbm{Q}_{p}}/\underline{\mathbbm{Z}_{p}})[-1]\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},

and

\mathbbm{Z}/p\otimes\mathbbm{Z}/p\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

The first one identifies
\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)=\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)^{\mathrm{nh}}
with
\underline{\Ext}(\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)^{\mathrm{nh}},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
and vice-versa, while the second one is the Pontryagin duality between the locally compact abelian groups
\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)^{\mathrm{lca}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)^{\mathrm{lca}}=\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)
.

Let us develop the tools necessary for the proof of
Lemma

4.4
.

Definition 4.5
.

An object
X\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
is
non-Hausdorff
if it satisfies
\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
. We define
\mathcal{NH}
as the full subcategory of
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
of non-Hausdorff objects.

Proposition 4.6
.

The following facts hold:

i)

\mathbf{FLCA}
is the full subcategory of
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
of the objects
X\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
such that
\underline{\Ext}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
.

ii)

Both
\mathbf{FLCA}
and
\mathcal{NH}
are stable by extensions in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. Moreover,
\mathbf{FLCA}
is stable by kernels and
\mathcal{NH}
is stable by cokernels.

iii)

We have
\mathcal{NH}\cap\mathbf{FLCA}=0
.

iv)

If we have
X\in\mathcal{NH}
and
Y\in\mathbf{FLCA}
, then we have
\Hom(X,Y)=0
.

v)

If
X\in\mathcal{NH}
, then so is
X^{\vee}[1]=\underline{\Ext}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
.

vi)

The Pontryagin duality
R\underline{\Hom}(-,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}):\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
induces equivalence of categories
\mathbf{FLCA}^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathbf{FLCA}
and
\mathcal{NH}^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{NH}[-1]
.

Proof.

To prove i), we just need to show that if
X\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
satisfies
\underline{\Ext}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
, then it is a locally compact abelian group. By
[
26
, Corollary 1.2.21]
, we have

X=\mathrm{cofib}(X^{-1}\rightarrow X^{0}),

where
X^{-1},X^{0}\in\mathbf{FLCA}
and the morphism
X^{-1}\rightarrow X^{0}
is a monomorphism. Consequently, we have an exact sequence in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{LCA})

0\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(X^{0},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(X^{-1},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

which tells us that
\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
is locally compact. Since
\underline{\Ext}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
, we have

X=R\underline{\Hom}(R\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=R\underline{\Hom}(\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}).

Since
\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
is locally compact, then we have
\underline{\Ext}(\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
and we conclude that

X=\underline{\Hom}(\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

is locally compact as well.

If we have
0\rightarrow X^{\prime}\rightarrow X\rightarrow X^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow 0
an exact sequence in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, we get a fibre sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})

X^{\prime\prime\vee}\rightarrow X^{\vee}\rightarrow X^{\prime\vee}.

If we take the long exact cohomology sequence, ii) follows.

For iii), we take
X\in\mathcal{NH}\cap\mathbf{FLCA}
. Then we have
X^{\vee}=R\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
. Consequently, we have
X=X^{\vee\vee}=0^{\vee}=0
. To prove iv), we observe that if
f:X\to Y
is a morphism in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
with
X\in\mathcal{NH}
and
Y\in\mathbf{FLCA}
, then the morphism
f^{\vee}:Y^{\vee}\rightarrow X^{\vee}
must be 0, since
Y^{\vee}
belongs to
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})^{\leq 0}
and
X^{\vee}
belongs to
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})^{\geq 1}
. Consequently, we have
f=f^{\vee\vee}=0
.

Let us prove v). Using
[
26
, Corollary 1.2.21]
, we represent
X\in\mathcal{NH}
as the cofiber in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
of the monomorphism
f:X^{-1}\to X^{0}
, where
X^{-1},X^{0}
are locally compact. Then we have a fibre sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})

(X^{0})^{\vee}\overset{f^{\vee}}{\rightarrow}(X^{-1})^{\vee}\rightarrow X^{\vee}[1].

Since we have
\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
, the morphism
f^{\vee}
is a monomorphism and
X^{\vee}[1]\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{LCA})
. Moreover, we have

\begin{split}\underline{\Hom}(X^{\vee}[1],\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})&=\underline{\Hom}(\underline{\Ext}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=\\
&=\mathrm{H}^{-1}(R\underline{\Hom}(R\underline{\Hom}(X,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=\mathrm{H}^{-1}(X)=0.\end{split}

As a consequence,
X^{\vee}[1]
is an object of
\mathcal{NH}
. Part vi) follows.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

Item iv) shows that for
X\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
it is enough to have no non-trivial continuous homomorphism
X\to\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
to guarantee that there are no non-trivial continuous homomorphisms
X\to A
, where
A
is locally compact.

Remark 4.8
.

By
[
26
, Corollary 1.2.21]
, for every object
X
of
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
we have a fibre sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})

X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow X,

where
X^{\prime},X^{\prime\prime}
are objects of
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
which are acyclic for
R\underline{\Hom}(-,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
. This is analogous to the two-term free resolution of an abelian group.

Proposition 4.9

(Decomposition in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
)
.

Let
A\in\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
be in the essential image of
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. Then we have a decomposition

0\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0,

where
A^{\mathrm{nh}}
is in the essential image of
\mathcal{NH}
and
A^{\mathrm{lca}}
is in the essential image of
\mathbf{FLCA}
. This decomposition is functorial in
A
and unique up to isomorphism.

Proof.

Let us prove the existence first. By hypothesis, we have
A=\underline{X}
, where
X\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
is the cofibre in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
of a monomorphism
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime\prime}
of locally compact abelian groups. We denote by
\overline{f(X^{\prime})}
the closure of
f(X^{\prime})
in
X^{\prime\prime}
, and we set

X^{\mathrm{nh}}\coloneqq\mathrm{cofib}(X^{\prime}\to\overline{f(X^{\prime})}),\qquad X^{\mathrm{lca}}\coloneqq\mathrm{cofib}(\overline{f(X^{\prime})}\rightarrow X^{\prime\prime}).

(4.3)

Then we have an exact sequence in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})

0\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow X\rightarrow X^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0.

We just need to show that we have
X^{\mathrm{nh}}\in\mathcal{NH}
and
X^{\mathrm{lca}}\in\mathbf{FLCA}
. Since
X^{\prime}
is dense in
\overline{f(X^{\prime})}
, every continuous morphism
\overline{f(X^{\prime})}\to\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
which vanishes on
X^{\prime}
is the zero morphism. This implies that the map
\underline{\Hom}(\overline{f(X^{\prime})},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\to\underline{\Hom}(X^{\prime},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
is a monomorphism in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. Consequently, we have
\underline{\Hom}(X^{\mathrm{nh}},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=0
, i.e.
X^{\mathrm{nh}}
is non-Hausdorff. Moreover, since the morphism
\overline{f(X)}\to X^{\prime\prime}
is a closed immersion, it is strict. By
[
26
, Proposition 1.2.29, a)]
, its cokernel computed in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
belongs to
\mathbf{FLCA}
. This proves that
X^{\mathrm{lca}}
belongs to
\mathbf{FLCA}
.

We now define
A^{\mathrm{nh}}\coloneqq\underline{X^{\mathrm{nh}}}=\underline{\overline{f(X^{\prime})}}/\underline{X^{\prime}}
and
A^{\mathrm{lca}}\coloneqq\underline{X^{\mathrm{lca}}}=\underline{X^{\prime\prime}}/\underline{\overline{f(X^{\prime})}}
. Since the functor
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})\rightarrow\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
is exact, we get the desired decomposition.

For uniqueness, let us consider another decomposition, say
0\to B\to A\to C\to 0
, with
B=\underline{Y}
and
C=\underline{Z}
,
Y\in\mathcal{NH}
and
Z\in\mathbf{FLCA}
. By fully faithfulness of the functor
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})\to\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
, and by item iv) of
Proposition

4.6
, we have

\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}(B,A^{\mathrm{lca}})=\Hom_{\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})}(Y,X^{\mathrm{lca}})=0.

Hence the composition
B\to A\to A^{\mathrm{lca}}
vanishes, inducing a morphism of exact sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\mathrm{nh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\mathrm{lca}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

By the Snake Lemma, the morphism
B\to A^{\mathrm{nh}}
is injective and the morphism
C\to A^{\mathrm{lca}}
is surjective. Moreover, the cokernel of
B\to A^{\mathrm{nh}}
coincides with the kernel of
C\to A^{\mathrm{lca}}
. By item ii) of
Proposition

4.6
, this cokernel is in the essential image of
\mathcal{NH}\cap\mathbf{FLCA}
, which is
0
by item iii) of
Proposition

4.6
. Consequently, both
B\to A^{\mathrm{nh}}
and
C\to A^{\mathrm{lca}}
are isomorphisms.

In the same way we prove the functoriality of the decomposition. Indeed, if we have
A,B\in\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
in the essential image of
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, let us consider the decompositions

0\rightarrow B^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow B\rightarrow B^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0,\quad 0\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0.

Since we have
\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}(B^{\mathrm{nh}},A^{\mathrm{lca}})=0
, we obtain a morphism between the exact sequences.
∎

Example 4.10
.

If
X=[\mathbbm{Z}\overset{\cdot p^{n}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbbm{Z}_{p}]
and
A=\underline{X}
, then the closure of
p^{n}\mathbbm{Z}
is
p^{n}\mathbbm{Z}_{p}
. Consequently, we have

A^{\mathrm{nh}}=\underline{p^{n}\mathbbm{Z}_{p}}/\underline{p^{n}\mathbbm{Z}},\quad A^{\mathrm{lca}}=\mathbbm{Z}/p^{n}.

Remark 4.11
.

The locally compact abelian group
A^{\mathrm{lca}}
is the maximal locally compact quotient of
A
. Indeed, for all
B\in\mathbf{LCA}
receiving a map from
A
, we have
\Hom(A^{\mathrm{nh}},B)=0
. Consequently, the map
A\to B
factors through
A^{\mathrm{lca}}
.

Remark 4.12
.

The decomposition
0\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0
plays for the Pontryagin duality in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
the same role that the decomposition
0\rightarrow M_{\mathrm{tors}}\to M\to M_{free}\to 0
plays for the
\mathbbm{Z}
-linear duality in
\mathbf{D}^{perf}(\mathbbm{Z})
.

Remark 4.13
.

If we consider the right t-structure on
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, and we denote its heart by
\mathcal{RH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, we get a full subcategory of
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
. In general, this is not contained in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
, since the t-structure on
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
does not coincide with the right t-structure on
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. Consequently, for
B\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
wihich is in the essential image of
\mathcal{RH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
we could have
\mathrm{H}^{1}(B)\neq 0
. Nonetheless, we obtain a similar unique decomposition as in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. In particular, for
B\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
which is in the essential image of
\mathcal{RH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, there exists a unique and functorial fibre sequence

B^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow B\rightarrow B^{\mathrm{nh}},

where we have
B^{\mathrm{lca}}\in\mathbf{FLCA}
and
B^{\mathrm{nh}}\in\mathcal{NH}[-1]
. Indeed, any object
Y\in\mathcal{RH}(\mathbf{LCA})
is the fibre of a continuous homomorphism
\pi:Y^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime\prime}
of locally compact abelian groups with a dense image. To obtain the decomposition, we set
B^{\mathrm{lca}}\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{Ker}(\pi)}\in\mathbf{FLCA}
and
B^{\mathrm{nh}}\coloneqq\underline{Y^{\prime\prime}}/\underline{\pi(Y^{\prime})}[-1]\in\mathcal{NH}[-1]
. This fibre sequence is unique, up to isomorphism.

The Pontryagin duality realises an equivalence of categories
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{RH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. We can see this fact under a new light. Indeed, these categories admit “decompositions”

\mathcal{NH}\rightarrow\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})\rightarrow\mathbf{FLCA},\qquad\mathbf{FLCA}\rightarrow\mathcal{RH}(\mathbf{FLCA})\rightarrow\mathcal{NH}[-1]

and the Pontryagin duality induces equivalences of categories
\mathcal{NH}^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{NH}[-1]
and
\mathbf{FLCA}^{\mathrm{op}}\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathbf{FLCA}
.

We are ready to prove
Lemma

4.4
.

Proof of
Lemma

4.4
.

The perfect pairing induces an equivalence

C\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}R\underline{\Hom}(D,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. We have a spectral sequence for the cohomology of
R\underline{\Hom}(D,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

E_{2}^{i,j}=\underline{\Ext}^{i}(\mathrm{H}^{-j}(D),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\implies\mathrm{H}^{i+j}(R\underline{\Hom}(D,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})).

Since we have
\mathrm{H}^{-j}(D)\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
for all
j
, the term
E_{2}^{i,j}
vanishes for all
i\neq 0,1
. Consequently, for all
n
, we have an exact sequence in
\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})

0\rightarrow\underline{\Ext}(\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{n}(R\underline{\Hom}(D,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}))\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow 0.

(4.4)

We observe that we have

\begin{split}\underline{\Ext}(\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})&=\underline{\Ext}(\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D)^{\mathrm{nh}},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=(\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D)^{\mathrm{nh}})^{\vee}[1]\in\mathcal{NH},\\
\underline{\Hom}(\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})&=\underline{\Hom}(\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)^{\mathrm{lca}},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})=(\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)^{\mathrm{lca}})^{\vee}\in\mathbf{FLCA}.\end{split}

We have for all
n

\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)\cong\mathrm{H}^{n}(R\underline{\Hom}(D,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})).

By uniqueness of the nh-lca decomposition, the exact sequence (
4.4
) induces isomorphisms

\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{nh}}\cong(\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D)^{\mathrm{nh}})^{\vee}[1]\cong(\mathrm{H}^{-n+1}(D)^{\mathrm{nh}}[-1])^{\vee},\qquad\mathrm{H}^{n}(C)^{\mathrm{lca}}\cong(\mathrm{H}^{-n}(D)^{\mathrm{lca}})^{\vee}

for all
n
. ∎

4.2
Duality with coefficients in tori

Let
T
be a torus over
F
and let
Y^{*}
be its Cartier dual. The bilinear map in
\mathbf{Ab}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
introduced in
Example

2.8

T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\times X^{*}(T)\rightarrow\overline{L}^{\times}.

induces a cup-product pairing

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times})[-1]=\mathbbm{Z}[-1].

(4.5)

Thanks to
[
3
, Theorem 4.27]
, we prove the following

Proposition 4.14

(Condensed duality for tori)
.

The cup-product pairing (
4.5
) is a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
.

In order to proceed with the proof, we need two preliminary results

Lemma 4.15
.

The canonical morphism

\varphi_{X^{*}(T)}:R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\rightarrow R\underline{\Hom}(R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T)),\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{Z})

is an equivalence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
.

Proof.

We have a fiber sequence

\tau^{\leq 1}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))[-2]

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
. By
[
3
, Theorem 3.30]
, we have

\tau^{\leq 1}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\in\mathbf{D}^{perf}(\mathbbm{Z})

and

\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))=(\mathbbm{Q}_{p}/\mathbbm{Z}_{p})^{n}\oplus H

for some
n\in\mathbbm{N}
and some finite abelian group
H
. Consequently, we have equivalences

\tau^{\leq 1}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}R\underline{\Hom}(R\underline{\Hom}(\tau^{\leq 1}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T)),\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{Z})

and

\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}R\underline{\Hom}(R\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T)),\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{Z}).

The result follows.
∎

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
M
be a free finitely generated abelian group with a continuous action of a finite quotient
G
of
G_{F}
. Then
M
is flat in
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
and the canonical morphism

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M)\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R})

is an equivalence in
\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{C})
.

Remark 4.17
.

In the statement of
Lemma

4.16
, the derived tensor products are computed in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
.

Remark 4.18
.

If
C\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
is such that
\mathrm{H}^{i}(C)
is a discrete torsion abelian group for all
i
, then we have
C\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}=0
in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
. To show this, we reduce to the case where
C=\mathbbm{Z}/n[0]
. We consider the flat resolution
\cdot n:\mathbbm{Z}\to\mathbbm{Z}
of
\mathbbm{Z}/n
. Then we have

\mathbbm{Z}/n\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}=[\mathbbm{Z}\otimes\mathbbm{R}\overset{\cdot n}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{Z}\otimes\mathbbm{R}],

which is equivalent to the
0
complex in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
.

Proof.

We first observe that we have
M_{|EG}\cong\mathbbm{Z}^{n}
for some
n\in\mathbbm{N}
, which is flat in
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}/EG
. Consequently,
M
is flat and we have
M\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}=M\otimes\mathbbm{R}
, which is a finitely generated
\mathbbm{R}
-vector space with a continuous action of
G
. By
[
3
, Proposition 2.30]
we have

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R})=R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},(M\otimes\mathbbm{R})^{H})

where
H
is the image of the inertia subgroup via the quotient
G_{F}\to G
.

On the other hand, we have a fiber sequence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},M^{H})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M)\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},\tau^{\geq 1}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{I}},M)).

By
[
3
, Example 2.42]
, we have

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},M^{H})=[M^{H}\overset{1-\varphi}{\longrightarrow}M^{H}]\in\mathbf{D}^{perf}(\mathbbm{Z})\subset\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA}).

Moreover, by
[
3
, Theorem 4.27]
, we have
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M)\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. Consequently, we have

C\coloneqq R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},\tau^{\geq 1}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{I}},M))\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA}).

Moreover, since
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{I}},M)=\underline{\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{I}(\mathbf{Set}),M)}
is torsion for all
q\geq 1
, by
Remark

4.18
, we have

C\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}=0

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
. Consequently,
the map

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},M^{H})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},M)\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}

is an equivalence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
. Since the complex
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},M^{H})
belongs to
\mathbf{D}^{perf}(\mathbbm{Z})
, we have
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},M^{H})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}=R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},M^{H})\otimes\mathbbm{R}
.

Therefore we need to check that the induced map

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},M^{H})\otimes\mathbbm{R}\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{W_{k}},(M\otimes\mathbbm{R})^{H})

is an equivalence. Both condensed abelian groups belong to
\mathbf{D}^{perf}(\mathbbm{R})
, hence it is enough to check that the morphism is an equivalence on the underlying abelian groups.
This reduces to the commutativity of the diagram of abelian groups

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{H}\otimes\mathbbm{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{H}\otimes\mathbbm{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M\otimes\mathbbm{R})^{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(M\otimes\mathbbm{R})^{H},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(1-\varphi)\otimes\mathbbm{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1-\varphi}

where the horizontal arrows are

(1-\varphi)\otimes\mathbbm{R}:M^{H}\otimes\mathbbm{R}\rightarrow M^{H}\otimes\mathbbm{R},\qquad m\otimes r\mapsto(m-\varphi(m))\otimes r,

1-\varphi:(M\otimes\mathbbm{R})^{H}\rightarrow(M\otimes\mathbbm{R})^{H},\qquad m\otimes r\mapsto m\otimes r-\varphi(m\otimes r)=m\otimes r-\varphi(m)\otimes r.

This is clear.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

4.14
.

Following the notations of
[
3
]
, we set

\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}(1)\coloneqq\mathrm{cofib}(\overline{L}^{\times}[-1]\overset{val}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{R}[-1])\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}}).

By
Proposition

2.9
, we have a fiber sequence

X^{*}(T)^{D}\rightarrow T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\mathbbm{R})

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
, where
X^{*}(T)^{D}\coloneqq R\underline{\Hom}(X(T),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}(1))
. We get a morphism of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X(T)^{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\mathbbm{R}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X(T)),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}[-2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T)),\mathbbm{R}[-1]),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\mathbbm{R}}}

where the middle map is the morphism

\psi_{T}:R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T)),\mathbbm{Z}[-1])

coming from (
4.5
). To show that
\psi_{T}
is an equivalence, it is enough to show that the same holds for
\psi_{D}
and
\psi_{\mathbbm{R}}
. The map
\psi_{D}
is an equivalence by
[
3
, Theorem 4.27]
. Concerning
\psi_{\mathbbm{R}}
, we have

\begin{split}R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T)),\mathbbm{R}[-1])&=R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1])=\\
&=R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T)\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1]),\end{split}

where the last equality is
Lemma

4.16
. By
[
3
, Lemma 3.19]
we have
R\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{R},\overline{L}^{\times})=0
, thus we obtain

(X(T)\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R})^{D}[1]=R\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),R\underline{\Hom}(\mathbbm{R},\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}(1)))[1]=\underline{\Hom}(X^{*}(T),\mathbbm{R}).

Consequently, the fact that
\psi_{\mathbbm{R}}
is an isomorphism follows from
[
3
, Theorem 4.27]
with
M=X^{*}(T)\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{R}
.

We just need to show that the other morphism induced from
equation

4.5
, i.e.

\psi_{X^{*}(T)}:R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))\rightarrow R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z}[-1])

is an equivalence. Up to equivalence, the map
\psi_{X^{*}(T)}
is given by

\begin{split}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T))&\overset{\varphi_{X*(T)}}{\longrightarrow}R\underline{\Hom}(R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},X^{*}(T)),\mathbbm{Z}[-1]),\mathbbm{Z}[-1])\overset{-\circ\psi_{T}}{\longrightarrow}\\
&\overset{-\circ\psi_{T}}{\longrightarrow}R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z}[-1]).\end{split}

The second morphism is an equivalence since
\psi_{T}
is. The first morphism is an equivalence by
Lemma

4.15
. Consequently, the morphism
\psi_{X^{*}(T)}
is an equivalence.

∎

4.3
Duality with coefficients in abelian varieties

Let
A
be an abelian variety over
F
, and let
A^{*}
be the dual abelian variety. As explained in the appendix (
Example

A.16
), the Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of
(A,A^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
induces a pairing
\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}:A\otimes^{L}A^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})
and a cup-product pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab})

R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A^{*}(\overline{F}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(G_{F}(\mathbf{Set}),\overline{F}^{\times})[-1]=\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1].

(4.6)

By
Theorem

A.11

\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a biextension of
(A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
, inducing a pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W_{F}}})

\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\otimes^{L}A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\overline{L}^{\times}[1]

and a condensed cup-product pairing in
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W_{F}}},\overline{L}^{\times})[0]=\mathbbm{Z}.

(4.7)

Moreover, the underlying pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab})
is compatible with (
4.6
).

We prove the Weil version of the Tate duality with coefficients in abelian varieties.

Theorem 4.19
.

(Tate duality for abelian varieties)
The cup-product pairing (
4.7
) is a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
and factors through
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1]
. In particular, for
q=0,1
, we get a perfect pairing of locally compact abelian group of finite ranks

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{1-q}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

Proof.

By
Proposition

3.9
, we have
R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{R})=0
, which implies that the map of (
4.7
) factors through
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1]
. Let us consider the map

\tau(A):R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1]).

Since the cohomology groups of the complexes
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
and
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
are either profinite or torsion, we observe that

•

\tau(A)
is an equivalence if and only if
\tau(A)(*)
is an equivalence in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab})
;

•

We have a canonical isomorphism

R\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\cong R\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}}(R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}).

Thus it is enough to show that the pairing

R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1]

is perfect.

By compatibility with (
4.6
) we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{F}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1].}

By
[
17
, Lemma 5.4.3]
and
Proposition

3.1
the left vertical arrow is an isomorphism. By Tate’s duality theorem with coefficients in abelian varieties (see
[
23
, Corollary 3.4]
) the upper pairing is perfect. The result follows.
∎

4.4
Duality with coefficients in
1
-motives

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
be a
1
-motive over
F
, where
E
is an extension of the abelian variety
A
by the torus
T
. In
[
9
, 10.2.11]
a dual
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}^{*}=[Y^{*}\overset{u^{*}}{\rightarrow}E^{*}]
is associated to
\mathcal{M}
, where
Y^{*}\coloneqq\mathcal{X}(T)
is the Cartier dual of
T
, while
E^{*}
is a semiabelian variety, extension of the dual abelian variety
A^{*}
of
A
by the torus
T^{*}
with Cartier dual
Y
. A Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}
of
(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
is defined. This gives a pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})

\psi_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1].

and a cup-product pairing for Galois cohomology

CP_{\mathcal{P}}:R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathcal{M}(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{F}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{2}(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\overline{F}^{\times})[-1]=\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[-1].

The construction of the dual
1
-motive, of the Poincaré pairing, as well as the compatibility with the weight filtration on
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{*}
are recalled in
Section

A.4.1
.
Moreover, in
Corollary

A.21
we show that
\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
, called
condensed Poincaré biextension
. We obtain a condensed Poincaré pairing

\psi_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\overline{L}^{\times}[1],

and a condensed cup-product pairing

CP_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times})=\mathbbm{Z}.

(4.8)

which are compatible with the condensed weight filtration as proved in
Lemma

A.30
.
We are ready to prove the following

Theorem 4.20

(Condensed duality with coefficients in
1
-motives)
.

The cup-product pairing (
4.8
) is a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
.

Proof.

By
Theorem

3.14
, we have
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
and
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
. We need to show that the morphisms induced by the cup-product pairing
CP_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\overset{\beta}{\rightarrow}R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})

are equivalences.

Reduction to the case
T=0
.
We have fiber sequences

T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}/T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},\qquad E^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow Y^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[1].

We denote by
\mathcal{P^{\prime}}
the Poincaré biextension of
(\mathcal{M}/T,E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. By
Lemma

A.30
a) and b) the map
\alpha
induces a morphism of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}/T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},Y^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})[1],\mathbbm{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime\prime}}

where
\alpha^{\prime}
, resp.
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
, is the morphism induced by the cup-product pairing (
4.5
), resp.
CP_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}
.
The morphism
\alpha
is an equivalence if and only if
\alpha^{\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
are. The same holds for
\beta
. Consequently, to show that
CP_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}
is a perfect pairing, it is enough to show that the cup-product pairings (
4.5
) and

CP_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}/T(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{Z}

are perfect. The first one is perfect by
Proposition

4.14
. The only thing left to show is that
CP_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}
is perfect.

The case
T=0
.
We reduced to the case
T=0
. We have fiber sequences

A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[1],\qquad T^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}.

By
Lemma

A.30
i) and ii)
\alpha
induces a morphism of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime\prime}}

where
\alpha^{\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
are induced by the cup-product pairings (
4.7
) and (
4.5
) (and the same for
\beta
). As before, it is enough to show that the cup-product pairings (
4.7
) and (
4.5
), i.e.

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{Z}

and

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow\mathbbm{Z}[-1],

are perfect. The first fact follows by
Theorem

4.19
, while the second is again
Proposition

4.14
.
∎

To express the duality as a Pontryagin duality on the cohomology groups, we use the
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
-twist defined in
Section

3.5
. Using this definition and
Lemma

4.4
we reformulate the duality as follows

Theorem 4.21
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a
1
-motive over
F
with dual
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}^{*}
. We have a perfect cup-product pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},

(4.9)

which induces perfect cup-product pairings in
\mathbf{FLCA}

\begin{split}\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{lca}}&\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},\\
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}}\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})&\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},\\
\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})&\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}\\
\end{split}

and a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{nh}}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

Proof.

The complex
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
is in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, hence we have

\begin{split}&R\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\cong\\
\cong&R\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{FLCA}}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\cong\\
\cong&R\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{FLCA}}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z})\cong R\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\mathbbm{Z}).\end{split}

Therefore, by
Theorem

4.20
, the pairing (
4.8
) induces an equivalence

\gamma:R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\rightarrow R\underline{\Hom}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
. Let us consider the cup-product pairing

cp_{\gamma}:R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
determined by this equivalence. This cup-product pairing is perfect. To see this, let us denote

A\coloneqq R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),\qquad B\coloneqq R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}).

Then
cp_{\gamma}
induces morphisms

\gamma_{A}:A\rightarrow R\underline{\Hom}(B,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\qquad\gamma_{B}:B\rightarrow R\underline{\Hom}(A,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}).

The morphism
\gamma_{A}
tautologically coincides with the morphism
\gamma
, which is an equivalence. Moreover, the morphism
\gamma_{B}
coincides, up to equivalence, to the composition

B\longrightarrow R\underline{\Hom}(R\underline{\Hom}(B,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}),\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\overset{-\circ\gamma}{\longrightarrow}R\underline{\Hom}(A,\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}).

The second morphism is an equivalence since
\gamma
is, while the first morphism is an equivalence since
B\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
is dualisable for the Pontryagin duality. Consequently,
\gamma_{B}
is an equivalence, and the cup-product pairing
cp_{\gamma}
is a perfect pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
and in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
.

The rest of the proof follows from the structure result on
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
, i.e.
Theorem

3.14
, and from
Lemma

4.4
.

∎

4.5
Comparison with Galois cohomology

We show how to deduce the first statement of
[
15
, Theorem 0.1]
from
Theorem

4.21
in the
p
-adic field case. To do this, we compare the Weil
\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}
-twisted cohomology
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})
with the Galois cohomology
R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{F})_{\mathrm{cond,\acute{e}t}})
.

We consider the condensed étale realisation and the condensed Weil-étale realisation of
Section

2

-(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}:\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}\rightarrow B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\qquad-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}:\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}\rightarrow B_{\hat{W}_{F}}.

The morphism of topoi
f:B_{\hat{W}_{F}}\rightarrow B_{\hat{G}_{F}}
induces a natural transformation

\eta:f^{*}\circ-(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}\implies-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}

of functors
\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}\to B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
. This natural transormation is not an isomorphism in general. For example, we have
f^{*}\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}=\overline{F}^{\times}
together with its
W_{F}
action induced by
G_{F}
and
\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=\overline{L}^{\times}
