. However,
\eta_{X}
is an isomorphism in some cases.

Example 4.22
.

Let
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
be a scheme which is locally constant for the étale topology, i.e. there exists
F^{\prime}
a finite extension of
F
such that
X_{F^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to the constant scheme
M_{F}
for some abelian group
M
. Then the map

\eta_{X}:f^{*}X(\overline{F})_{\acute{e}t,cond}\rightarrow X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}

is an isomorphism in
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
, and both condensed
\hat{W}_{F}
-modules coincide with the discrete abelian group
X(\overline{F})=X(\overline{L})=M
with its action of
G_{F}
which factors through
\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)
(see
Example

2.7
).

Let
G\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
be a commutative group scheme over
F
which is locally of finite type. Composing the canonical map

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},G(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},f^{*}G(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})

with the map induced by
\eta_{G}
, we obtain a canonical morphism

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},G(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},G(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}).

The same holds if we replace
G
by a
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}
.

Before studying the properties of this morphism, we analyse the structure of the condensed cohomology groups
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},G(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})
in the case where
G
is either an algebraic group or a commutative group scheme which is étale locally constant.

Proposition 4.23
.

1)

Let
G
be a commutative group scheme locally of finite type over
F
. The canonical map

\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),G(\overline{F}))\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},G(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})

is an isomorphism of abelian groups for all
q
.

2)

If
E
is a semiabelian variety, the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})
is a discrete torsion abelian group for all
q\geq 1
. Consequently, it coincides with the discrete abelian group
\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),E(\overline{F}))
for all
q\geq 1
.

3)

If
Y
is étale locally constant, the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},Y(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})
is discrete for all
q\geq 0
and torsion for all
q\geq 1
. Consequently, it coincides with the discrete abelian group
\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),Y(\overline{F}))
for all
q
.

4)

If
\mathcal{M}
is a
1
-motive over
F
and
q\geq 1
, the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})
is discrete torsion, and coincides with
\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathcal{M}(\overline{F}))
.

Proof.

The proof of 1) is exactly the same of
Proposition

3.1
.

The proof of 2) is an easier analogue of the proof of
Proposition

3.9
. In particular, the result is the consequence of the following facts

(i)

For all
q
, the condensed abelian group
\prescript{}{m}{\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})}
is discrete and finite.

(ii)

For all
q\geq 1
, the abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})(S)
is torsion for all
S
extremally disconnected.

To prove (i), we observe that for all
m\in\mathbbm{N}
we have an exact sequence in
\mathbf{Gp}_{F}

0\rightarrow E_{m}\rightarrow E\rightarrow E\rightarrow 0,

where
E_{m}
is a finite group scheme. By
Propositions

3.10
and
2.10
we have

E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m=E_{m}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}[1],

which is a finite Galois module. The Kummer sequence

0\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q-1}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},E_{m}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\rightarrow\prescript{}{m}{\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})}\rightarrow 0

implies that
\prescript{}{m}{\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})}
is finite for all
q
.

To prove (ii), it is enough to show that for all
F^{\prime}
finite extension of
F
, the abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)},\underline{E(F^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}})
is torsion. By
[
3
, Proposition 2.41]
we have

\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)},\underline{E(F^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}}})(S)=\mathrm{H}^{q}(B_{\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)}(\mathbf{Set}),\Cont(S,E(F^{\prime})^{\mathrm{top}})).

The result follows since higher cohomology groups of the finite group
\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)
are killed by the order of
\Gal(F^{\prime}/F)
.

The proof of 3) follows from
[
3
, Remark 3.22]
with
I
replaced by
G_{F}
. Item 4) follows from 1), 2) and 3).
∎

We analyse the relation between the condensed Galois cohomology and the condensed Weil cohomology of
1
-motives.

Lemma 4.24
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\to E]
be a
1
-motive over
F
. Then for all
m\in\mathbbm{N}
the canonical morphism

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m

is an equivalence of complexes with finite cohomology groups in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
.Consequently, we obtain an equivalence

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
.

Proof.

Since we have

\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}=[Y(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}\rightarrow E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}],\qquad\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}=[Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}],

it is enough to show the result for
\mathcal{M}=[Y\to 0]
and for
\mathcal{M}=[0\to E]
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\to 0]
. The morphism

f^{*}Y(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}\to Y(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is an isomorphism since
Y
is étale locally constant. Consequently, it is enough to show that the canonical morphism

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},M\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m)\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},f^{*}(M\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m))

is an equivalence of complexes with finite cohomology groups for
M=Y(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}
. Since
M
is free and finitely generated with a continuous action of a finite quotient of
G_{F}
, then
M\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m
is a finite
G_{F}
-module, and the result follows from
[
3
, Proposition 3.26]
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[0\to E]
.
As we already observed, we have

E(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m=E_{m}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}[1]

and

E(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m=E_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}[1].

Consequently, it is enough to show that the canonical morphism

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},E_{m}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},E_{m}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})

is an equivalence of complexes with finite cohomology groups. Again, this follows from the fact that
E_{m}
is étale locally constant and from
[
3
, Proposition 3.26]
.
∎

Corollary 4.25
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\to E]
be a
1
-motive over
F
. For all
m\in\mathbbm{N}
, we have a canonical equivalence

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m[1]

of complexes with finite cohomology groups in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
. Consequently, we obtain an equivalence

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[1]

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{LCA})
.

Proof.

We remark that for every
M\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{FLCA})
we have

M\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})}\mathbbm{Z}/m=M\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{FLCA}}\mathbbm{Z}/m=\mathrm{cofib}(M\overset{\cdot m}{\rightarrow}M).

Consequently, we have

\begin{split}R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m&=R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{FLCA}}(\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbf{FLCA}}\mathbbm{Z}/m)=\\
&=R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m[1].\end{split}

The result now follows from
Lemma

4.24
.
∎

Remark 4.26
.

We recall that if
C\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})
, we have a Kummer sequence

0\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(C)/m\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(C\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Z}/m)\rightarrow\prescript{}{m}{\mathrm{H}^{q+1}(C)}\rightarrow 0.

If we take the filtered colimit along integers
m
, we obtain an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(C)\otimes\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(C\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q+1}(C)_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow 0

(4.10)

If
\prescript{}{m}{\mathrm{H}^{q}(C)}
is finite for all
m
, taking the cofiltered limit along
m
gives an exact sequence (according to the diagram
[
3
, (4.5)]
)

0\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(C)^{\wedge}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})\rightarrow T\mathrm{H}^{q+1}(C)\rightarrow 0.

(4.11)

Remark 4.27
.

Let us consider the equivalence

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[1].

By
Proposition

4.23
, the condensed abelian group
\mathbbm{H}^{q}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})
are torsion for
q=1,2
. Consequently, using the exact sequence (
4.10
) we obtain an identification

\mathrm{H}^{0}(R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}[1])=\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}).

and an exact sequence

\begin{split}0&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\otimes\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow\\
&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow 0.\end{split}

(4.12)

We are ready to prove the following corollary to
Theorem

4.21

Corollary 4.28
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
be a
1
-motive over
F
, and let
\mathcal{M}^{*}=[Y^{*}\overset{u^{*}}{\rightarrow}E^{*}]
be its Cartier dual. The perfect pairing (
4.9
) induces a perfect cup-product pairing in
\mathbf{FLCA}

\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(F,\mathcal{M})_{\wedge}\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{2}(F,\mathcal{M}^{*})\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},

(4.13)

where we set

\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(F,\mathcal{M})_{\wedge}\coloneqq\mathrm{Ker}((\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge}\rightarrow(\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge})

and

\mathbbm{H}^{2}(F,\mathcal{M}^{*})\coloneqq\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}).

Proof.

We set

\begin{split}C&\coloneqq\mathrm{cofib}(\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}),\\
D&\coloneqq\mathrm{fib}(\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}/T^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}))\end{split}

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C}))
. By
Theorem

4.21
, we have
C^{\vee}=D
. Consequently, we have

C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}=(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\vee}[1].

Let us consider the exact sequences (
4.11
)

0\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(C)^{\wedge}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})\rightarrow T\mathrm{H}^{q+1}(C)\rightarrow 0

and (
4.10
)

0\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(D)\otimes\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q}(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{q+1}(D)_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow 0.

We observe that
\mathrm{H}^{q}(C)^{\wedge}
and
T\mathrm{H}^{q+1}(C)
are profinite, while
\mathrm{H}^{q}(D)\otimes\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}
and
\mathrm{H}^{q+1}(D)_{\mathrm{tors}}
are torsion abelian groups. In particular, they are locally compact. Since
\mathbf{LCA}
is stable by extensions in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{C})
(
[
3
, Proposition 4.5]
), both condensed abelian groups
\mathrm{H}^{q}(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})
and
\mathrm{H}^{q}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})
are locally compact. By
Lemma

4.4
, the equivalence

C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}=(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\vee}[1].

gives a perfect cup-product pairing in
\mathbf{LCA}

\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}.

We show that we have

\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})=\mathrm{Ker}((\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge}\rightarrow(\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge}),\qquad\mathrm{H}^{0}(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})=\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}).

Firstly, we have a fibre sequence in
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{C})

\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}\rightarrow\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}\rightarrow C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}.

By (
4.11
) we observe that both
\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}
and
\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}}
are concentrated in cohomological degrees
0
and
1
. The equality

\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})=\mathrm{Ker}((\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge}\rightarrow(\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge})

follows from the long exact cohomology sequence

T(\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})\to T(\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})\to\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})\to(\underline{Y(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge}\to(\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})^{\wedge}\to\mathrm{H}^{0}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})\to 0.

Indeed, the structure theorem of
\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}}
(see
Proposition

3.11
) implies the vanishing of
T(\underline{E(F)^{\mathrm{top}}})
.

Let us consider the exact sequence in
\mathbf{FLCA}

0\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)\otimes\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow 0.

By definition of
D
, the long exact cohomology sequence associated to

R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},T^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow R\mathbb{\Gamma}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}/T^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})

gives an epimorphism

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)

and an isomorphism

\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}).

By
Theorem

4.21
, we have

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\otimes\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}=(T\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}})^{\vee}=0,

where the vanishing of the Tate module follows from the structure of
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}})
, see
Theorem

3.14
. This implies that

\mathrm{H}^{0}(D)\otimes\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z}=0,

and we have

\mathrm{H}^{0}(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})=\mathrm{H}^{1}(D)_{\mathrm{tors}}=\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}.

The result now follows from the exact sequence (
4.12
), which gives the identification

\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})_{\mathrm{tors}}=\mathbbm{H}^{2}(B_{\hat{G}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{F})_{cond,\acute{e}t}).

∎

We conclude with a further analysis of the perfect pairing

\mathbbm{H}^{1}(F,\mathcal{M})_{\wedge}\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{2}(F,\mathcal{M}^{*})\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},

whose induced pairing on the underlying abelian groups is item 1 of
[
15
, Theorem 2.3]
. First, we make the following observation.

Remark 4.29
.

If
A\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
, then the condensed abelian groups
\prescript{}{m}{A}
and
A/m
are finite for all
m
. This can be seen, for example, by taking the
2
-term resolution

A_{0}\rightarrow A_{1}\rightarrow A,

with
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
locally compact of finite ranks. If moreover
A
is non-Hausdorff, we have
A/m\in\mathcal{NH}\cap\mathbf{FLCA}=0
(see
Proposition

4.6
, ii) and iii)). It follows that for all
A\in\mathcal{LH}(\mathbf{FLCA})
the derived completion of the canonical decomposition

0\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0

gives an exact sequence of profinite abelian groups

0\rightarrow TA^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow TA\rightarrow TA^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0

and an isomorphism

A^{\wedge}\cong(A^{\mathrm{lca}})^{\wedge}.

Applying this observation to
\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
, we obtain

T\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})=T\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}

and

\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\wedge}=(\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{lca}})^{\wedge}.

We recall that the cup-product pairing (
4.13
) can be expressed as

\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}(D\otimes^{L}\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{Q}/\mathbbm{Z},

where
C
and
D
are defined in the proof of
Corollary

4.28
.

Applying
Remark

4.29
to the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C)^{\wedge}\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{-1}(C\otimes^{L}\hat{\mathbbm{Z}})\rightarrow T\mathrm{H}^{0}(C)\rightarrow 0,

we obtain an exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\wedge}\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(F,\mathcal{M})_{\wedge}\rightarrow T\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow 0.

(*)

Moreover, taking the torsion of the canonical decomposition

\begin{split}0&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{nh}}\rightarrow\\
&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{lca}}\rightarrow 0\end{split}

gives the exact sequence

\begin{split}0&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{nh}}_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow\\
&\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{2}(F,\mathcal{M}^{*})\rightarrow\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{lca}}_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow 0.\end{split}

(**)

Combining (
*
‣
4.5
) and (
**
‣
4.5
), we observe that the perfect cup-product pairing (
4.13
) can be decomposed into the two perfect cup-product pairings

\mathbbm{H}^{-1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\wedge}\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{lca}}_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z}

and

T\mathbbm{H}^{0}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})^{\mathrm{nh}}\otimes\mathbbm{H}^{1}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}};\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z})^{\mathrm{nh}}_{\mathrm{tors}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}/\mathbbm{Z},

which can be immediately deduced from
Theorem

4.21
.

Appendix A
Biextensions and pairings

The goal of this appendix is to prove the existence of some condensed cup-product pairings which are needed in
Section

4
. In particular we need a condensed Poincaré pairing for the Weil-étale realisation of abelian varieties and for the Weil-étale realisation of 1-motives. To do so, we use the notion of
biextensions
developed in
[
14
, VII]
. To show that these pairings are compatible with the non-condensed ones, we recall some results on cup-product pairings and their compatibility with respect to morphisms of topoi.

A.1
Cup-product pairings

Let
\mathscr{T}
be a topos and let
X,Y\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T})
. We denote by
\Gamma(\mathscr{T},-):\mathscr{T}\rightarrow\mathbf{Set}
the global section functor. There is a canonical pairing in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab})

\varphi_{X,Y}:R\Gamma(\mathscr{T},X)\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T},Y)\rightarrow R\Gamma(\mathscr{T},X\otimes^{L}Y)

(A.1)

which can be described via the cup-product pairings of abelian groups

\varphi_{X,Y}^{r,s}:\mathrm{H}^{r}(\mathscr{T},X)\otimes\mathrm{H}^{s}(\mathscr{T},Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{r+s}(\mathscr{T},X\otimes^{L}Y)

as follows. We have a natural isomorphism of abelian groups

\mathrm{H}^{r}(\mathscr{T},X)=\Hom_{\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T})}(\mathbbm{Z},X[r])

and similarly for
Y
and
X\otimes^{L}Y
. We represent elements
\alpha\in\mathrm{H}^{r}(\mathscr{T},X)
and
\beta\in\mathrm{H}^{s}(\mathscr{T},Y)
as homotopy classes of maps
\alpha:\mathbbm{Z}\to I_{X}[r]
and
\beta:\mathbbm{Z}\to I_{Y}[s]
, where
I_{X}
and
I_{Y}
are injective resolutions of
X
and
Y
respectively. Then
\varphi^{r,s}_{X,Y}(\alpha\otimes\beta)
is represented by

\mathbbm{Z}=\mathbbm{Z}\otimes\mathbbm{Z}\rightarrow I_{X}[r]\otimes I_{Y}[s]\overset{\sim}{\leftarrow}P(I_{X})\otimes I_{Y}[r+s],

where
P(I_{X})
is a flat resolution of
I_{X}
.

Definition A.1
.

Let
X,Y,Z\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T})
and let
\psi:X\otimes^{L}Y\rightarrow Z
be a morphism in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T})
. We define the cup-product pairing induced by
\psi
as the composition

R\Gamma(\mathscr{T},X)\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T},Y)\overset{\varphi_{X,Y}}{\longrightarrow}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T},X\otimes^{L}Y)\overset{R\Gamma(\mathscr{T},\psi)}{\longrightarrow}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T},Z)

(A.2)

and we denote it
CP_{\psi}
.

Let
f:\mathscr{T}_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}_{2}
be a morphism of topoi. The left adjoint
f^{*}
is exact and induces a functor

f^{*}:\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{2})\rightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{1}).

For
X,Y\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{2})
, we have a canonical morphism

R\Hom_{\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{2})}(X,Y)\rightarrow R\Hom_{\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{1})}(f^{*}X,f^{*}Y)

(A.3)

functorial in both terms and described as follows. Represent an element
\alpha:R\Hom_{\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{2})}(X,Y)
as the homotopy class of a map
\alpha:X\to I_{Y}
. Since
f^{*}
is exact,
f^{*}I_{Y}
is a resolution of
f^{*}Y
. The image of
\alpha
is represented by

f^{*}X\overset{f^{*}\alpha}{\longrightarrow}f^{*}I_{Y}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}I_{f^{*}Y},

where
I_{f^{*}Y}
is an injective resolution of
f^{*}I_{Y}
.
For
X=\mathbbm{Z}
, we get the canonical morphism

R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{2},Y)\rightarrow R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}Y)

which is functorial in
Y
.

Let
\psi:X\otimes^{L}Y\rightarrow Z
and
\psi^{\prime}:f^{*}X\otimes^{L}f^{*}Y\rightarrow f^{*}Z
be morphisms in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{2})
and
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{1})
respectively. Then we have a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{2},X)\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{2},Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{2},Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}X)\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}Z).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{\psi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{\psi^{\prime}}}

(A.4)

Definition A.2
.

We say that
\psi
and
\psi^{\prime}
induce compatible cup-product pairings if (
A.4
) is commutative.

Remark A.3
.

For
X,Y\in\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{2})
, we have a canonical isomorphism

f^{*}X\otimes^{L}f^{*}Y\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}f^{*}(X\otimes^{L}Y).

This follows from the isomorphism between non-derived objects and from flatness of
f^{*}P
for a flat
P
(see
[
2
, IV.13.4 and V.1.7.1]
).

Consequently, given a pairing
\psi:X\otimes^{L}Y\rightarrow Z
in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{2})
we have an induced pairing

f^{*}\psi:f^{*}X\otimes^{L}f^{*}Y\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}f^{*}(X\otimes^{L}Y)\rightarrow f^{*}Z

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{1})
.

Proposition A.4
.

The morphisms
\psi
and
f^{*}\psi
induce compatible cup-product pairings.

Proof.

We decompose the diagram (
A.4
) as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{2},X)\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{2},Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{2},X\otimes^{L}Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{2},Z)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}X)\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}(X\otimes^{L}Y))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}Z),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{X,Y}}

where the first arrow on the second line is the composite

R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}X)\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}Y)\overset{\varphi_{f^{*}X,f^{*}Y}}{\longrightarrow}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}X\otimes^{L}f^{*}Y)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}R\Gamma(\mathscr{T}_{1},f^{*}(X\otimes^{L}Y)).

The commutativity of the right square follows from functoriality of (
A.3
) in the second argument. We are left to show the commutativity of the left square, which is clear from the definitions of
\varphi_{X,Y}^{r,s}
and
\varphi_{f^{*}X,f^{*}Y}^{r,s}
.
∎

A.2
Biextensions in a topos

The definitions and results of this section are mostly contained in
[
14
, VII]
.
In what follows,
\mathscr{T}
denotes a topos. If
X,Y\in\mathscr{T}
, we denote by
X_{Y}\in\mathscr{T}_{/Y}
the inverse image of
X
via the localisation morphism
j_{Y}:\mathscr{T}_{/Y}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}
, i.e. the object
X\times Y\rightarrow Y
. We recall the definitions of a torsor and of a biextension in
\mathscr{T}
, as well as the relation between biextensions and pairings.

Definition A.5
.

Let
G
be a group object of
\mathscr{T}
, and let
T\rightarrow X
be a morphism in
\mathscr{T}
. We say that
T
is a
G_{X}
-torsor over
X
if we have an action of
G_{X}
on
T
over
X
which is locally trivial, i.e. there exists a covering
\{U_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
in
\mathscr{T}
such that for all
i
the base change
T\times_{X}U_{i}
is isomorphic to
G\times U_{i}
, where the action of
G
is given by the left multiplication.

Remark A.6
.

If
\pi:T\to X
is a
G_{X}
-torsor over
X
, then
\pi
is an epimorphism in
\mathscr{T}
. Indeed, by definition there is a covering
\{U_{i}\to X\}_{i\in I}
such that the restriction
\pi_{|U_{i}}:T_{|U_{i}}\cong G\times U_{i}\rightarrow U_{i}
is an epimorphism for all
i
. Since the morphism
\coprod U_{i}\rightarrow X
is an epimorphism, the localisation functor
j:\mathscr{T}_{/\coprod U_{i}}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}_{/X}
is such that
j^{*}
is conservative. The result follows.

For any
X\in\mathscr{T}
and any
G\in\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T})
, we denote by
\mathbf{Tors}(X,G_{X})
the category of
G_{X}
-torsors over
X
, where morphisms of torsors are morphisms over
X
compatible with the action of
G_{X}
.

Definition A.7
.

Let
A,B
and
G
be abelian group objects of
\mathscr{T}
. A
biextension
of
(A,B)
by
G
is an object
W
of
\mathscr{T}
together with a surjective morphism
\pi:W\rightarrow A\times B
endowed with the following structure:

a)

An action
W\times_{A\times B}G_{A\times B}\rightarrow W
of
G_{A\times B}
on
W
making
W
into a
G_{A\times B}
-torsor.

b)

An
A
-morphism
m_{A}:W\times_{A}W\rightarrow W
and a section
e_{A}:A\rightarrow W
of the structure morphism
W\to A
, making
W
into an abelian group object of
\mathscr{T}_{/A}
.

c)

A
B
-morphism
m_{B}:W\times_{B}W\rightarrow W
and a section
e_{B}:B\rightarrow W
of the structure morphism
W\to B
, making
W
into an abelian group object of
\mathscr{T}_{/B}
.

These structures are to satisfy the following conditions:

i)

if
G_{A}\rightarrow W
is the map
(g,a)\mapsto e_{A}(a)\cdot g
, then the sequence

0\rightarrow G_{A}\rightarrow W\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}B_{A}\rightarrow 0

is an exact sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{/A})
.

ii)

if
G_{B}\rightarrow W
is the map
(g,b)\mapsto e_{B}(b)\cdot g
, then the sequence

0\rightarrow G_{B}\rightarrow W\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}A_{B}\rightarrow 0

is an exact sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{/B})
.

iii)

(compatibility between
m_{A}
and
m_{B}
) the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(W\times_{A}W)\times_{B\times B}(W\times_{A}W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\times_{B}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(W\times_{B}A)\times_{A\times A}(W\times_{B}W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\times_{A}W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{A}\times_{B\times B}m_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{B}\times_{A\times A}m_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{A}}

where the isomorphism on the left is
(w_{1},w_{2};w_{3},w_{4})\mapsto(w_{1},w_{3};w_{2},w_{4})
.

For any
A,B,G\in\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T})
we denote by
\mathbf{Biext}(A,B;G)
the category of biextensions of
(A,B)
by
G
, where morphisms of biextensions are morphisms in
\mathscr{T}
which yield both a morphism of extensions over
A
and over
B
, as defined in
[
14
, VII,2.3]

In
[
14
, VII,2.5]
, a product of biextensions is defined. This makes the monoid of endomorphism of some
W\in\mathbf{Biext}(A,B;G)
canonically isomorphic to the monoid of endomorphism of the trivial biextension
A\times B\times G
. We denote this monoid by
\mathrm{Biext}^{0}(A,B;G)
and we observe that it is an abelian group. Moreover, there is a canonical bijection of abelian groups (
[
14
, VII,2.5.4]
)

\mathrm{Biext}^{0}(A,B;G)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T})}(A\otimes B,G),\quad[u]\mapsto\psi_{u}

(A.5)

described as follows. Let
\pi:W\to A\times B
be a biextension of
(A,B)
by
G
. Every automorphism
u
of
W
is in particular an automorphism of the underlying
G_{A\times B}
-torsor, and it is induced by a morphism

u_{0}:A\times B\to G

via the formula
u(x)=u_{0}(\pi(x))\cdot x
. In order for
u
to be an automorphism of biextensions,
u_{0}
needs to be bilinear. Consequently, it defines the desired morphism
A\otimes B\to G
.

Let
\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(A,B;G)
denote the set of isomorphism classes of biextensions of
(A,B)
by
G
. The product defined in
[
14
, VII,2.5]
makes it an abelian group. One has a canonical isomorphism of abelian groups (
[
14
, VII, Corollary 3.6.5]
)

\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(A,B;G)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T})}(A\otimes^{L}B,G[1]),\quad[W]\mapsto\psi_{W}.

(A.6)

Remark A.8
.

(Functoriality) The isomorphisms (
A.5
) and (
A.6
) are functorial

a)

(
[
14
, VII, 3.7]
) in
A,B
and
G
. Let
f:A^{\prime}\to A
and
g:B^{\prime}\to B
be morphisms in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T})
. One has a canonical functor

-\times_{A\times B}(A^{\prime}\times B^{\prime}):\mathbf{Biext}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathbf{Biext}(A^{\prime},B^{\prime};G)

inducing morphisms of abelian groups
\mathrm{Biext}^{i}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathrm{Biext}^{i}(A^{\prime},B^{\prime};G)
for
i=0,1
. Functoriality in
A
and
B
is the commutativity of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Biext}^{i}_{\mathscr{T}}(A,B;G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T})}(A\otimes^{L}B,G[i])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Biext}^{i}_{\mathscr{T}}(A^{\prime},B^{\prime};G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T})}(A^{\prime}\otimes^{L}B^{\prime},G[i]).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\times_{A\times B}(A^{\prime}\times B^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\circ(f\otimes g)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

b)

(
[
14
, VII,2.8, VIII,2.4]
) with respect to the change of topoi. Let
f:\mathscr{T}_{1}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}_{2}
be a morphism of topoi. Since biextensions are defined using finite limits and colimits, we have a canonical functor

f^{*}:\mathbf{Biext}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathbf{Biext}(f^{*}A,f^{*}B;f^{*}G)

which induces homomorphisms of abelian groups
\mathrm{Biext}^{i}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathrm{Biext}^{i}(f^{*}A,f^{*}B;f^{*}G)
for
i=0,1
. By
Remark

A.3
, we also have a morphism of abelian groups

f^{*}-:\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T}_{2})}(A\otimes^{L}B,G[i])\rightarrow\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T}_{1})}(f^{*}A\otimes^{L}f^{*}B,f^{*}G[i]),\qquad\psi\mapsto f^{*}\psi.

for
i=0,1
. The functoriality consists in the commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Biext}^{i}_{\mathscr{T}_{2}}(A,B;G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T}_{2})}(A\otimes^{L}B,G[i])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Biext}^{i}_{\mathscr{T}_{1}}(f^{*}A,f^{*}B;f^{*}G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T}_{1})}(f^{*}A\otimes^{L}f^{*}B,f^{*}G[i])}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

for
i=0,1
. By
Proposition

A.4
if
[u]\in\mathrm{Biext}^{0}(A,B;G)
(resp. if
[W]\in\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(A,B,G)
),
\psi_{u}
and
\psi_{f^{*}u}
(resp.
\psi_{W}
and
\psi_{f^{*}W}
) induce compatible cup-product pairings.

A.3
Biextensions of commutative group schemes

Let
F
be a
p
-adic field. We recall that
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}}
and
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}
denote the fppf topos over
F
and the big étale topos over
F
respectively. We have a morphism of topoi

g:\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}}\rightarrow\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}.

For all
X\in\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}
and
A,B,G\in\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}})
we have functors

\mathbf{Tors}(X,G_{X})\rightarrow\mathbf{Tors}(g^{*}X,g^{*}G_{g^{*}X}),\quad\mathbf{Biext}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathbf{Biext}(g^{*}A,g^{*}B;g^{*}G).

Proposition A.9
.

Let
X
be an
F
-scheme locally of finite type and let
G
be an affine commutative algebraic group over
F
. Let
T
be a
G_{X}
-torsor over
X
for the fppf topology. Then we have

(i)

T
is representable.

(ii)

there exists an étale cover
\{f_{i}:U_{i}\rightarrow X\}
of
X
which trivialises
T
as a
G_{X}
-torsor.

In particular, the functor

\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}(X,G_{X})\rightarrow\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(X,G_{X})

is an equivalence of categories, and the morphism

\mathrm{Tors}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}(X,G_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{Tors}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(X,G_{X})

is an isomorphism of abelian groups.

Proof.

The proof of (i) and (ii) is contained in
[
24
, III, Section 4]
. The functor

\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}(X,G_{X})\rightarrow\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(X,G_{X})

is fully faithful by (i), and it is essentially surjective by (ii).
∎

Corollary A.10
.

Let
A,B
be commutative group schemes which are locally of finite type over
F
, and let
G
be an affine commutative algebraic group over
F
. Let
W
be a biextension of
(A,B)
by
G
for the fppf topology. Then we have

(i)

W
is representable

(ii)

W
is a biextension of
(A,B)
by
G
in
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}
.

In particular, the functor

\mathbf{Biext}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathbf{Biext}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(A,B;G)

is an equivalence of categories, and the morphism

\mathrm{Biext}^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathrm{Biext}^{i}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(A,B;G)

is an isomorphism of abelian groups for
i=0,1
.

Proof.

By
Proposition

A.9
,
W
is representable and it is a
G_{A\times B}
-torsor over
A\times B
for the étale topology. To show that
W
is a biextension of
(A,B)
by
G
in
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}
, the only non-trivial conditions to check are i) and ii) of
Definition

A.7
. The surjectivity of
\pi:W\to A\times B
for the étale topology is guaranteed by the fact that this morphisms realises
W
as a
G_{A\times B}
-torsor over
A\times B
(see
Remark

A.6
). We need to show that

\mathrm{Ker}(W\rightarrow A_{B})=G_{B}

in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}{}_{/B})
. We obtain the isomorphism after application of
g^{*}
, which is fully faithful on representable objects of
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}{}/B
. Items (i) and (ii) follow.

Item (i) assures that the functor

\mathbf{Biext}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathbf{Biext}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(A,B;G)

is fully faithful, and item (ii) assures its essential surjectivity.
∎

As a consequence, when
A,B,G
are as in the hypotheses of
Corollary

A.10
, the study of étale biextensions and fppf biextensions is equivalent. We call such objects just biextensions of
(A,B)
by
G
, without specifying the topology.

The cohomology of
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}
coincides with Galois cohomology. Consequently, any biextension
W
of
(A,B)
by
G
, resp. any automorphism
u
of a biextension, induces a canonical cup-product pairing

CP_{W}:R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),B(\overline{F}))\rightarrow R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),G(\overline{F})[1]),

resp.

CP_{u}:R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),B(\overline{F}))\rightarrow R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),G(\overline{F})).

which is compatible with the correspondent fppf-pairing by
Remark

A.8
, b).

A.3.1
The Weil-étale realisation of biextensions

In this section we let
A,B
be commutative group schemes locally of finite type over
F
, and we let
G
be a commutative affine algebraic group over
F
.

The Weil-étale realisation functor

-(\overline{L}):\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}\rightarrow B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\qquad M\mapsto M(\overline{L})

respects products and sends étale coverings to epimorphic families of
B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})
. In order to see this last point, it is enough to observe that if
U\rightarrow X
is an étale surjective morphism in
\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
, then the morphism
U(\overline{L})\to X(\overline{L})
is surjective as well. Since étale surjective morphisms are stable under base change, the morphism
U_{L}\to X_{L}
is étale and surjective. Consequently, the induced morphism on
\overline{L}
-points is surjective. Therefore, the Weil-étale realisation induces a morphism of topoi

f_{W,\acute{E}t}:B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})\rightarrow\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}},

such that
f^{*}_{W,\acute{E}t}(M)=M(\overline{L})
for every
M\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
. Consequently, we have a functor

\mathbf{Biext}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathbf{Biext}(A(\overline{L}),B(\overline{L});G(\overline{L})),\quad W\mapsto f^{*}_{W,\acute{E}t}(W)=W(\overline{L})

which induces homomorphisms of abelian groups

\mathrm{Biext}^{i}(A,B;G)\rightarrow\mathrm{Biext}^{i}(A(\overline{L}),B(\overline{L});G(\overline{L}))

for
i=0,1
. By
Remark

A.8
, b), a biextension
W
and its Weil-étale realisation
W(\overline{L})
induce compatible cup-product pairings, i.e. the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),B(\overline{F}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),G(\overline{F}))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{L}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),B(\overline{L}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),G(\overline{L}))[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W(\overline{L})}}

(A.7)

is commutative. Similarly, an automorphism
u
of a biextension
W
and its Weil-étale realisation induce compatible cup-product pairings.

A.3.2
The condensed Weil-étale realisation of biextensions

Let
A,B
and
G
as in the previous section. To define the condensed Weil-étale realisation of a biextension, the first idea would be to follow the previous section, where the role of the Weil-étale realisation is taken by the condensed Weil-étale realisation
-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}:\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}\rightarrow B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
defined in
Section

2
. However, it is not clear (for the moment) whether the condensed Weil-étale realisation of an étale surjective map
U\rightarrow X
gives an epimorphism in
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
. Nonetheless, we are still able to prove the following in the case where
G=\mathbbm{G}_{m}

Theorem A.11
.

Let
A,B
be commutative group schemes locally of finite type over
F
, and let
W
be a biextension of
(A,B)
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. Then
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a biextension of
(A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},B(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
.

If
W^{\prime}
is another biextension of
(A,B)
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
and
u:W\to W^{\prime}
is a morphism of biextensions, then
u(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}:W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\to W^{\prime}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a morphism of biextensions.

We need the following

Lemma A.12
.

Let
\{f_{i}:X_{i}\rightarrow X\}_{i\in I}
be a local section cover of topological spaces, i.e. a collection of continuous maps such that
\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}\to X
admits local sections. Then the map
\coprod_{i\in I}\underline{X_{i}}\rightarrow\underline{X}
is an epimorphism in
\mathscr{C}
.

Proof.

Since the condensification functor
\underline{(-)}:\mathbf{Top}\to\mathscr{C}
respects coproducts (see for example
[
3
, Remark 2.32]
), we have

\coprod_{i\in I}\underline{X_{i}}=\underline{\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}}.

Let
S
be a compact Hausdorff topological space and let
f:S\rightarrow X
be a continuous map. We need to show that there exists
S^{\prime}
compact Hausdorff together with a continuous surjection
S^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow S
and a continuous map
g:S^{\prime}\rightarrow\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
such that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

By hypothesis, there exists an open covering
\{U_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of
X
such that
p_{|p^{-1}(U_{j})}:h^{-1}(U_{j})\to U_{j}
admits a section
s_{j}
. For all
j\in J
, we set
V_{j}\coloneqq f^{-1}(U_{j})
. By compactness,
S
is covered by a finite number of such
f^{-1}(U_{j})
’s. By regularity of
S
, we can refine
V_{j}
to a finite covering
\{S_{1},\dots,S_{n}\}
of closed subsets of
S
such that for all
k=1,\dots n
, we have
S_{k}\subset f^{-1}(U_{j_{k}})
for some
j\in J
. The result now follows by setting

S^{\prime}\coloneqq S_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup S_{n},\qquad g_{k}:S_{k}\rightarrow\sqcup_{i\in I}X_{i},\qquad g_{k}\coloneqq s_{j_{k}}\circ f,\,\forall k=1,\dots,n.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

A.11
.

Firstly, we set
X\coloneqq A\times_{F}B
and we show that
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a
\overline{L}^{\times}_{X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}
-torsor over
X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
. To do this, we exhibit an epimorphic family
\{X_{i}\rightarrow X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\}
in
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
which realises

W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\times_{X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}X_{i}\cong\overline{L}^{\times}\times X_{i}.

(A.8)

Let
\{U_{i}\rightarrow X\}
be an étale cover of
X
which trivialises
W
in
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}
. Since the condensed Weil-étale realisation functor respects fiber products, the condition (
A.8
) is satisfied with
X_{i}\coloneqq U_{i}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
. It remains to show that
\{U_{i}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\}
is an epimorphic family. By
[
13
, XI, Proposition 5.1]
, we can suppose
\{U_{i}\to X\}
to be a Zariski cover of
X
. Consequently, for all
K
finite extension of
L
, we get an open cover
\{U_{i}(K)^{\mathrm{top}}\rightarrow X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}\}
. By
Lemma

A.12
, the morphism

\coprod_{i}\underline{U_{i}(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}

is an epimorphism after localisation
j^{*}_{EW_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)}:B_{W_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
. By conservativity of
j^{*}_{EW_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)}
, we conclude that
\{\underline{U_{i}(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}\rightarrow\underline{X(K)^{\mathrm{top}}}\}
is a cover in
B_{W_{F}/\Gal(\overline{L}/K)}
. Since the colimit of epimorphisms is an epimorphism, the family
\{U_{i}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow X(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\}
is epimorphic. This shows that
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a
\overline{L}^{\times}_{A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\times B(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}
-torsor. In particular, the morphism
\pi:W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\times B(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is an epimorphism in
B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
. The other conditions that
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
needs to satisfy in order to be a biextension of
(A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},B(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
are expressed as fiber products. Since the condensed Weil-étale realisation functor respects fiber products, we conclude.

In order to prove the last statement, we let
W,W^{\prime}
be biextensions of
(A,B)
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. In order for a morphisms
W\to W^{\prime}
to be a morphism of biextensions, it needs to satisfy conditions that only involve fiber products. Since the Weil-étale realisation functor respects fiber products, the result follows.
∎

Remark A.13
.

The fact that
G=\mathbbm{G}_{m}
is crucial since there exists a Zariski cover of
A\times B
which trivialises
W
as a
\mathbbm{G}_{m,A\times B}
-torsor (see
[
13
, XI, Proposition 5.1]
). For more general affine
G
, the same proof holds for those biextension
W
of
(A,B)
by
G
that can be trivialised by a Zariski cover of
A\times_{F}B
.

We introduce a morphism of topoi
\alpha:B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})\rightarrow B_{\hat{W}_{F}}
as follows. For every finite extension
K
of
L
, we set
U_{K}\coloneqq\Gal(\overline{L}/K)
. We have a morphism of topoi
\beta_{K}:B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}\rightarrow B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}(\mathbf{Set})
whose right adjoint

\beta_{K*}:B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}\rightarrow B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}(\mathbf{Set}),\quad M\mapsto M(*)

sends a condensed
W_{F}/U_{K}
-module to its underlying
W_{F}/U_{K}
-set. Since the functor
-(*):\mathscr{C}\to\mathbf{Set}
commutes with all limits and colimits, then so does the functor
\beta_{K*}
. Consequently, there is a section of
\beta_{K}

\alpha_{K}:B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}(\mathbf{Set})\rightarrow B_{W_{F}/U_{K}}.

with
\alpha_{K}^{*}=\beta_{K*}
. Taking the inverse limit over finite extensions of
L
, we obtain morphisms

\beta:B_{\hat{W}_{F}}\rightarrow B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\qquad\alpha:B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})\rightarrow B_{\hat{W}_{F}},

such that
\alpha
is a section of
\beta
. Moreover, the composition

\alpha^{*}\circ-(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}:\mathbf{Sch}_{F}^{\mathrm{lft}}\rightarrow B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})

coincides with the Weil-étale realisation
-(\overline{L}):\mathbf{Sch}_{F}^{\mathrm{lft}}\to B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})
.

Proposition A.14
.

Let
A,B
be commutative group schemes locally of finite type over
F
, and let
W
be a biextension of
(A,B)
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. Then
W
, its Weil-étale realisation
W(\overline{L})
and its condensed Weil-étale realisation
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
induce compatible cup-product pairings. In other words, the following diagram has commutative squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),B(\overline{F}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{F}))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{L}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),B(\overline{L}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L}))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},B(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W(\overline{L})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proof.

We have morphisms of topoi

f_{W,\acute{e}t}:B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})\rightarrow\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}},\qquad\alpha:B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})\rightarrow B_{\hat{W}_{F}}.

The biextension
W(\overline{L})
coincides with the inverse image of
W
via
f_{W,\acute{e}t}
and also with the inverse image of
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
via
\alpha
. We conclude by
Remark

A.8
, b).
∎

Remark A.15
.

In the lower commutative square the vertical arrows are isomorphisms. This follows from
Proposition

3.1
, or equivalently by the exactness of
\beta_{*}=\alpha^{*}:B_{\hat{W}_{F}}\rightarrow B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set})
.

Example A.16
.

(The Poincaré biextension,
[
23
, Example C.1]
)
Let
A
be an abelian variety over
F
and
A^{*}
be its dual abelian variety. Then there exists an essentially unique biextension
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of
(A,A^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
such that the correspondent pairing

A\otimes^{L}A^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})
identifies
A^{*}
with
\underline{\Ext}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(A,\mathbbm{G}_{m})
. We call this extension
Poincaré biextension
. By
Proposition

A.14
we get a diagram with commutative squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A^{*}(\overline{F}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{F})[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A(\overline{L}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),A^{*}(\overline{L}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L}))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},A^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times})[1].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{L})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{\mathcal{P}_{0}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Here the upper-left vertical morphism is an isomorphism by
[
18
, Lemma 5.4.3]
.

A.4
Biextensions of 1-motives

In
[
9
]
, Deligne generalises the concept of biextension in a topos
\mathscr{T}
to two-term complexes as follows.

Definition A.17
.

Let
K_{1}=[A_{1}\rightarrow B_{1}]
and
K_{2}=[A_{2}\rightarrow B_{2}]
be two complexes concentrated in degrees
-1
and
0
in
C^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}))
, and let
H
be an abelian group object of
\mathscr{T}
. A biextension
W
of
(K_{1},K_{2})
by
H
consists in

a)

A biextension
W_{1}
of
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
by
H
;

b)

A trivialisation (i.e. a biadditive section) of the biextension
W_{1}^{\prime}
of
(A_{1},B_{2})
by
H
obtained from
W_{1}
by pullback along
A_{1}\times B_{2}\to B_{1}\times B_{2}
.

c)

A trivialisation of the biextension
W^{\prime\prime}_{1}
of
(B_{1},A_{2})
by
H
obtained from
W_{1}
by pullback along
B_{1}\times A_{2}\to B_{1}\times B_{2}
.

We ask that the two trivialisations coincide on
A_{1}\times A_{2}
. If we denote by
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
the trivialisations, this means that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\times B_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\prime}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}\times A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{1}\times A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\prime\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}}

commutes.

Definition A.18
.

Let
W
be a biextension of
(K_{1},K_{2})
by
H
as in
Definition

A.17
. An automorphism of
W
is given by an automorphism of
W_{1}
, say
\varphi:W_{1}\rightarrow W_{1}
, which is compatible with the biadditive sections
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
, i.e. such that
\varphi\circ s_{i}=s_{i}
for
i=1,2
.

The monoid of automorphisms of any
W
is canonically isomorphic to the monoid of automorphisms of the trivial biextension. We denote it by
\mathrm{Biext}^{0}(K_{1},K_{2};H)
and we observe that it is an abelian group. We have a canonical isomorphism of abelian groups (
[
9
, 10.2.1]
)

\mathrm{Biext}^{0}(K_{1},K_{2};H)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\Hom_{\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T})}(\mathrm{H}^{0}(K_{1})\otimes\mathrm{H}^{1}(K_{2}),H)

(A.9)

that can be described as follows. Every automorphism of
W
is given by an automorphism
\varphi
of
W_{1}
compatible with the biadditive sections. As before,
\varphi
is determined by a biadditive map
u_{0}:B_{1}\times B_{2}\rightarrow H
. This defines a morphism
B_{1}\otimes B_{2}\to H
. The compatibility with the biadditive sections
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
precisely states that the restriction of
u_{0}
to
A_{1}\times B_{2}
and
B_{1}\times A_{2}
is the zero morphism. Hence the morphism induced by
u_{0}
factors through
\mathrm{H}^{0}(K_{1})\otimes\mathrm{H}^{0}(K_{2})
.

The set of isomorphism classes of biextensions of
(K_{1},K_{2})
by
H
has an abelian group structure, and we denote it by
\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(K_{1},K_{2};H)
. As in (
A.6
), we have an isomorphism

\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(K_{1},K_{2};H)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{\mathbf{D}(\mathscr{T})}(K_{1}\otimes^{L}K_{2},H[1]).

(A.10)

Remark A.19
.

The same functoriality of
Remark

A.8
holds for (
A.9
) and (
A.10
).

Remark A.20
.

We study three special cases:

a)

if
K_{1}=[0\to B_{1}]
and
K_{2}=[0\to B_{2}]
, then (
A.9
) and (
A.10
) canonically identify with (
A.5
) and (
A.6
).

b)

If
K_{1}=[A_{1}\to 0]
and
K_{2}=[0\to B_{2}]
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Biext}^{0}(A_{1},B_{2};H)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(K_{1},K_{2};H)

which sends the class of a biadditive map
\sigma:A_{1}\times B_{2}\to H
to the class of the couple

(W_{1}\to 0\times B_{2},s:A_{1}\times B_{2}\to W_{1}),

where
W_{1}\coloneqq 0\times B_{2}\times H
is the trivial biextension and the biadditive section
s:A_{1}\times B_{2}\to W_{1}
is given by
(0\times\mathrm{id}_{B_{2}},\sigma)
. Under (
A.9
) and (
A.10
), this isomorphism identifies with

-[1]:\Hom(A_{1}\otimes^{L}B_{2},H)\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}{\Hom}(K_{1}\otimes^{L}K_{2},H[1]).

c)

Let
K_{1}=[A_{1}\overset{u_{1}}{\to}B_{1}]
and
K_{2}=[0\to B_{2}]
. By the previous items we have an exact sequence of abelian groups

\mathrm{Biext}^{0}(A_{1},K_{2};H)\overset{\alpha}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(K_{1},K_{2};H)\overset{\beta}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Biext}^{1}(B_{1},K_{2};H).

Here
\alpha
and
\beta
can be described as follows

•

\alpha
sends the class of
\sigma:A_{1}\times B_{2}\to H
to the class of

(W_{1}\to B_{1}\times B_{2},s:A_{1}\times B_{2}\to W_{1}),

where
W_{1}\coloneqq B_{1}\times B_{2}\times H
is the trivial biextension and
s:A_{1}\times B_{2}\to W_{1}
is the biadditive section
(u_{1}\times\mathrm{id}_{B_{2}},\sigma)
.

•

\beta
sends the class of
(W_{1}\to B_{1}\times B_{2},\sigma:A_{1}\times B_{2}\to W_{1})
to the class of
W_{1}
.

Under (
A.9
) and (
A.10
), this exact sequence canonically identifies with the usual exact sequence

\Hom(A_{1}\otimes^{L}B_{2},H)\rightarrow\Hom(K_{1}\otimes^{L}B_{2},H[1])\rightarrow\Hom(B_{1}\otimes^{L}B_{2},H[1]).

This generalisation to two-term complexes applies to
1
-motives. Let
\mathcal{M}_{1}=[Y_{1}\rightarrow E_{1}]
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=[Y_{2}\rightarrow E_{2}]
be
1
-motives over a
p
-adic field
F
, as defined in
Section

2.3
. If
H
is smooth and affine, it is equivalent to study biextensions of
(\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2})
by
H
in
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}}
and
\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}}
by
Corollary

A.10
. Moreover, any such biextension is representable. We use
Theorem

A.11
to prove the following

Corollary A.21
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{1}=[Y_{1}\to E_{1}]
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}=[Y_{2}\to E_{2}]
be two
1
-motives over the
p
-adic field
F
, and let
W
be a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. Then
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
.

Proof.

Let
W_{1},s_{1}
and
s_{2}
be respectively the biadditive section of
(E_{1},E_{2})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, the biadditive section
Y_{1}\times E_{2}\rightarrow W_{1}
and the biadditive section
E_{1}\times Y_{2}\rightarrow W_{1}
which define
W
.

By
Theorem

A.11
, the morphism

W_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow E_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\times E_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}

realises
W_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
as a biextension of
(E_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},E_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
. Moreover, since the Weil-étale realisation functor respects products, the morphisms
s_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
and
s_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
are biadditive sections, and they coincide on
Y_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\times Y_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
. Consequently, the data of
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\coloneqq(W_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},s_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}),s_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
define a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
.
∎

Remark A.22
.

Let
W
be a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
.
By
[
14
, VIII,3.5]
, its restriction
W_{1}
to
E_{1}\times E_{2}
is the pullback of a biextension
W_{0}
of
(A_{1},A_{2})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
along the morphism
E_{1}\times E_{2}\rightarrow A_{1}\times A_{2}
. Thus
W_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is the pullback of
W_{0}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
, biextension of
(A_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},A_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
.

As a consequence of
Corollary

A.21
, if
W
is a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{2})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
inducing a pairing

\psi_{W}:\mathcal{M}_{1}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}_{2}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})
(or
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{\acute{E}t}})
), we get a biextension
W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
inducing a pairing

\psi_{W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}:\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}\rightarrow\overline{L}^{\times}[1]

in
\mathbf{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(B_{\hat{W}_{F}})
. As in Section
A.3.1
, we also have an induced biextension
W(\overline{L})
of
(\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{L}),\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{L}))
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})
and a pairing

\psi_{W(\overline{L})}:\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{L})\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{L})\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L})[1].

By adapting the proof of
Proposition

A.14
, one shows that the induced cup-product pairings are compatible, i.e. we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{F}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{F}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{G_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{F}))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{L}))\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{L}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{W_{F}}(\mathbf{Set}),\mathbbm{G}_{m}(\overline{L}))[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})\otimes^{L}R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\mathcal{M}_{2}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B_{\hat{W}_{F}},\overline{L}^{\times})[1]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W(\overline{L})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces CP_{W(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

A.4.1
The Poincaré biextension

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\to}E]
be a
1
-motive over
F
. In
[
9
, 10.2.11]
a dual
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}^{*}=[Y^{*}\overset{u^{*}}{\to}E^{*}]
and a biextension
\mathcal{P}
of
(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
are constructed. This biextension is such that its restriction to
E\times E^{*}
is the pullback of the Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of
(A,A^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
discussed in
Example

A.16
. We call
\mathcal{P}
Poincaré biextension as well. We recall these constructions and we give a proof of the well-known fact that
\mathcal{P}
is compatibile with the weight filtration.

Construction
(Case
T=0
). We suppose firstly
T=0
. Then we have
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}A]
. We define the dual
1
-motive
\mathcal{M}^{*}\coloneqq[0\rightarrow E^{*}]
, where
E^{*}
is the algebraic group representing the fppf-sheaf
X\mapsto\Ext^{1}_{X}(\mathcal{M}_{|X},\mathbbm{G}_{m|X})
. In particular, an element of
E^{*}(X)
is a couple
(\mathcal{E},\eta)
, where
\mathcal{E}
is an extension of
A_{X}
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}
and
\eta
is a trivialisation of
\mathcal{E}
along
Y_{X}\overset{u_{X}}{\rightarrow}A_{X}
. If we set
T^{*}\coloneqq\underline{\Hom}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(Y,\mathbbm{G}_{m})
and
A^{*}\coloneqq\underline{\Ext}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(A,\mathbbm{G}_{m})
, we have an exact sequence in
\mathbf{Ab}(\mathscr{T}_{\mathrm{fppf}})

0\rightarrow T^{*}\rightarrow E^{*}\rightarrow A^{*}\rightarrow 0.

The two maps can be described as follows

\begin{split}T^{*}(X)=\Hom_{X}(Y_{X},\mathbbm{G}_{m,X})\rightarrow E^{*}(X)=\Ext_{X}(\mathcal{M}_{X},\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}),\qquad&\varphi\mapsto(A_{X}\times\mathbbm{G}_{m,X},u_{X}\times\varphi),\\
E^{*}(X)=\Ext_{X}(\mathcal{M}_{X},\mathbbm{G}_{m,X})\rightarrow A^{*}(X)=\Hom_{X}(A_{X},\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}),\qquad&(\mathcal{E},\eta)\mapsto\mathcal{E}.\end{split}

Let
\mathcal{P}_{0}
be the Poincaré biextension of
(A,A^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. For all
X
and for all
\mathcal{E}\in A^{*}(X)
we denote by
\mathcal{P}_{0,\mathcal{E}}
the pullback
\mathcal{P}_{0}\times_{A^{*}}X
. By definition of
\mathcal{P}_{0}
we have
\mathcal{P}_{0,\mathcal{E}}=\mathcal{E}
, which is an extension of
A_{X}
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}
.

The pullback of
\mathcal{P}_{0}\to A\times A^{*}
by the map
A\times E^{*}\rightarrow A\times A^{*}
defines a biextension of
(A,E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. We denote it by
\mathcal{P}_{1}
. In order to define a biextension of
(\mathcal{M},E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, we have to exhibit a biadditive section
Y\times E^{*}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}
. We firstly observe that for all
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
and for all
(\mathcal{E},\eta)\in E^{*}(X)
, we have

\mathcal{P}_{1,(\mathcal{E},\eta)}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{1}\times_{E^{*}}X=(\mathcal{P}_{0}\times_{A^{*}}E^{*})\times_{E^{*}}X=\mathcal{P}_{0,\mathcal{E}}=\mathcal{E},

which is an extension of
A_{X}
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}
. Moreover,
\eta
gives a trivialisation of such extension along
Y_{X}\to A_{X}
. Indeed, we have the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

We define a biadditive section
\sigma:Y\times E^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}
as follows. For all
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
, we set

\sigma(X):Y(X)\times E^{*}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}(X),\quad(y,(\mathcal{E},\eta))\mapsto\eta(X)(y)\in\mathcal{E}(X)=\mathcal{P}_{1,(\mathcal{E},\eta)}(X)\subset\mathcal{P}_{1}(X).

(A.11)

Definition A.23
.

(Case
T=0
) The Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}
of
(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
consists in the following data:

•

the biextension
\mathcal{P}_{1}
of
(A,E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, pullback of
\mathcal{P}_{0}
, the Poincaré biextension of
(A,A^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
.

•

the biadditive section
\sigma:Y\times E^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}
defined in
equation

A.11
.

Remark A.24
.

We determine the restriction of
\sigma:Y\times E^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}
to
Y\times T^{*}
. We fix
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
. Following (
A.11
) and composing with the morphism
T^{*}(X)\to E^{*}(X)
we have

\sigma_{|}(X):Y(X)\times T^{*}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}(X),\qquad(y,\varphi)\mapsto(u(X)(y),\varphi(X)(y))\in A(X)\times\mathbbm{G}_{m}(X).

Thus
\sigma_{|Y\times T^{*}}
is determined by the biadditive map
C_{\sigma}:Y\times T^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}
given for
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
by

C_{\sigma}(X):Y(X)\times T^{*}(X)\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}(X),\quad(y,\varphi)\mapsto\varphi(X)(y).

C_{\sigma}
is exactly
\gamma_{T^{*}}
, the map inducing Cartier duality between
Y
and
T^{*}
(see
Example

2.8
).

Compatibility with weight filtration
(Case
T=0
). The Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}
induces a morphism
\psi_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
. The weight filtrations on
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{*}
reduce to the fiber sequences

A\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\rightarrow Y[1]

and

T^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow A^{*}

respectively. We have the following maps:

•

\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}:A\otimes^{L}A^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
, the map induced by the Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of
(A,A^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
.

•

\gamma_{T^{*}}:Y\otimes^{L}T^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, induced by the biadditive map of Cartier duality
Y\times T^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, which coincides with
C_{\sigma}
by
Remark

A.24
.

Compatibility of
\psi_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
with weight filtration is expressed by the following

Lemma A.25
.

Let
\mathcal{M}=[Y\to A]
be a
1
-motive (with
T=0
) and let
\mathcal{P}
be the Poincaré biextension of
(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, inducing
\psi_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
. The following facts hold:

a)

The composition
A\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\overset{\psi_{\mathcal{P}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
factors through
A\otimes^{L}A^{*}
. The induced map
A\otimes^{L}A^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
is
\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
.

b)

The composition
\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}T^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{*}\overset{\psi_{\mathcal{P}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
factors through
Y[1]\otimes^{L}T^{*}
. The induced map
Y\otimes^{L}T^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}
is
\gamma_{T^{*}}
.

This result is well-known. However, the author could not find a proof in the literature that could be adapted directly to the condensed setting.

Proof.

By functoriality in the first two terms of the isomorphism (
A.10
) (see
Remark

A.19
), the composition
A\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\overset{\psi_{\mathcal{P}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
coincides with
\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}:A\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
. In order to prove a) it is enough to show that
\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}
is the image of
\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}:A\otimes^{L}A^{*}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
via the morphism

\Hom(A\otimes^{L}A^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1])\rightarrow\Hom(A\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]).

Since by definition
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is the pullback of
\mathcal{P}_{0}
along
A\times E^{*}\to A\times A^{*}
, the result follows again by functoriality of (
A.10
).

As before, the composition
\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}T^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\overset{\psi_{\mathcal{P}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
identifies with
\psi_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}
, where
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
is the biextension of
(\mathcal{M},T^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
given by
(\mathcal{P}_{0}\times_{A\times A^{*}}A\times T^{*},\sigma_{|Y\times T^{*}})
. We need to show that
\psi_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}
is the image of
\gamma_{T^{*}}
via the morphism

\Hom(Y\otimes^{L}T^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m})\rightarrow\Hom(\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}T^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]).

The map
T^{*}\to A^{*}
is trivial, hence
\mathcal{P}_{0}\times_{A\times A^{*}}A\times T^{*}
is the trivial biextension. Using the identification of
Remark

A.20
, c), we conclude that
\psi_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}
is the image of
C_{\sigma}:Y\times T^{*}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m}
via

\Hom(Y\otimes^{L}T^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m})\rightarrow\Hom(\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}T^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]).

We conclude since
C_{\sigma}=\gamma_{T^{*}}
by
Remark

A.24
.
∎

Construction
(General case). We have
\mathcal{M}=[Y\overset{u}{\rightarrow}E]
. We define the dual motive
\mathcal{M}^{*}\coloneqq[Y^{*}\overset{u^{*}}{\rightarrow}E^{*}]
, with

Y^{*}=\underline{\Hom}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(T,\mathbbm{G}_{m}),\quad E^{*}=\underline{\Ext}_{\mathrm{fppf}}(\mathcal{M}/T,\mathbbm{G}_{m}).

The morphism
u^{*}
is defined as follows. We consider
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
and
\chi:T_{X}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}
, an element of
Y^{*}(X)
. By pushing out
E_{X}
along
\chi
we obtain an extension of
A_{X}
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}
, say
\mathcal{E_{\chi}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\chi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\chi}}

(A.12)

Moreover, the composition
\overline{\chi}\circ u_{X}:Y_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\chi}
defines a trivialisation of the extension
\mathcal{E}_{\chi}
along the morphism
\pi_{X}\circ u_{X}:Y_{X}\to A_{X}
. The morphism
u^{*}(X)
is thus defined as follows

u^{*}(X):Y^{*}(X)\rightarrow E^{*}(X),\quad\chi\mapsto(\mathcal{E}_{\chi},\overline{\chi}\circ u_{X}).

The previous procedure (where we supposed
T=0
) defines a biextension
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
of
(\mathcal{M}/T,E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, given by a biextension
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}
of
(A,E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
and a biadditive section
\sigma^{\prime}:Y\times E^{*}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}
. The pullback of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}
along the morphism
E\times E^{*}\rightarrow A\times E^{*}
defines a biextension
\mathcal{P}_{1}
of
(E,E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
. Moreover, the biadditive section
\sigma^{\prime}:Y\times E^{*}\to\mathcal{P^{\prime}}_{1}
factors through
\mathcal{P}_{1}
giving
\sigma:Y\times E^{*}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}
. In order to define a biextension of
(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, we have to exhibit a biadditive section
\tau:E\times Y^{*}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}
. We firstly observe that for all
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
and for all
(\mathcal{E},\eta)\in E^{*}(X)
, we have

\mathcal{P}_{1,(\mathcal{E},\eta)}=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{A\times E^{*}}(E\times X)=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1,(\mathcal{E},\eta)}\times_{A_{X}}E_{X}=\mathcal{E}\times_{A_{X}}E_{X}\eqqcolon\tilde{\mathcal{E}}.

Consequently, we have the following pullback diagram of exact sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X}}

and
\mathcal{P}_{1,(\mathcal{E},\eta)}=\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
is an extension of
E_{X}
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}
.

If
(\mathcal{E},\eta)\in E^{*}(X)
is in the essential image of
u^{*}(X):Y^{*}(X)\to E^{*}(X)
, then it is of the form
(\mathcal{E}_{\chi},\overline{\chi}\circ u_{X})
for some
\chi\in Y^{*}(X)
. Consequently we have a trivialisation of
\mathcal{E}
given by
\overline{\chi}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{E}_{\chi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{\chi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\chi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{X}}

This allows us to define, for all
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
the biadditive section

\tau(X):E(X)\times Y^{*}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}(X),\quad(e,\chi)\mapsto(\overline{\chi}(X)(e),e)\in\mathcal{E}_{\chi}\times_{A(X)}E(X)=\tilde{\mathcal{E}_{\chi}}(X)\subset\mathcal{P}_{1}(X).

(A.13)

Definition A.26
.

The Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}
of
(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
consists in the following data

•

the biextension
\mathcal{P}_{1}
of
(E,E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
, pullback of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}
, the Poincaré biextension of
(A,E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
presented in
Definition

A.23
.

•

the biadditive section
\sigma:Y\times E^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{1}
induced by
\sigma^{\prime}:Y\times E^{*}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}
defined in (
A.11
).

•

the biadditive section
\tau:E\times Y^{*}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}
defined in (
A.13
).

Remark A.27
.

We determine the restriction of the biadditive section
\tau:E\times Y^{*}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}
to
T\times Y^{*}
. We fix
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
. By definition, for all
t\in T(X)
and all
\chi\in Y^{*}(X)=\Hom(T_{X},\mathbbm{G}_{m,X})
we have

\tau(X)(t,\chi)=(\overline{\chi}(X)(t),u^{*}_{X}(\chi))\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(X).

Since the restriction of
\overline{\chi}:E_{X}\to\mathcal{E}_{\chi}
to
T_{X}
is exactly
\chi:T_{X}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m,X}
,
\tau_{|T\times Y^{*}}(X)
is given by

\tau_{|}(X):T(X)\times Y^{*}(X)\to\mathcal{P}_{1}(X),\qquad(t,\chi)\mapsto(\chi(X)(t),u^{*}_{X}(\chi)).

We have an induced biadditive map
C_{\tau}:T\times Y^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}
given for all
X\in\mathbf{Sch}^{\mathrm{lft}}_{F}
by

C_{\tau}(X):T(X)\times Y^{*}(X)\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}(X),\qquad(t,\chi)\mapsto\chi(X)(t).

C_{\tau}
is exactly
\gamma_{T}
, the map inducing Cartier duality between
T
and
Y^{*}
(see
Example

2.8
).

Compatibility with weight filtration
(General case). The Poincaré biextension induces a morphism
\psi_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
. The weight filtration on
\mathcal{M}
, resp.
\mathcal{M}^{*}
, gives fiber sequences

T\rightarrow E\rightarrow A,\qquad E\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\rightarrow Y[1],

and respectively

T^{*}\rightarrow E^{*}\rightarrow A^{*},\qquad E^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow Y^{*}[1].

We have the following maps:

•

\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}:A\otimes^{L}A^{*}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
induced by the Poincaré biextension
\mathcal{P}_{0}
of
(A,A^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
.

•

\gamma_{T}:T\otimes^{L}Y^{*}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m}
induced by Cartier duality, which coincides with
C_{\tau}
by
Remark

A.27
.

•

\gamma_{T^{*}}:Y\otimes^{L}T^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}
induced by Cartier duality, which coincides with
C_{\sigma}
by
Remark

A.24
.

Compatibility of
\psi_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
with weight filtration is expressed by the following

Lemma A.28
.

The following facts hold:

a)

The composition
\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}E^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\overset{\psi_{\mathcal{P}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
factors through
\mathcal{M}/T\otimes^{L}E^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
. The induced map
\mathcal{M}/T\otimes^{L}E^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
is
\psi_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
.

b)

The composition
T\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\overset{\psi_{\mathcal{P}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
factors through
T\otimes^{L}Y^{*}[1]
. The induced map
T\otimes^{L}Y^{*}\rightarrow\mathbbm{G}_{m}
is
\gamma_{T}
.

Remark A.29
.

By combining this with
Lemma

A.25
, we obtain that the map
\psi_{\mathcal{P}}
induces the maps
\psi_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}
,
\gamma_{T}
and
\gamma_{T^{*}}
on the graded pieces of the weight filtration. Again, this result is well-known but the author could not find a satisfying proof directly adaptable to the condensed setting.

Proof.

Let us start with a). As in the proof of
Lemma

A.25
, by functoriality of
equation

A.10
the image of
\psi_{\mathcal{P}}
via

\Hom(\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1])\rightarrow\Hom(\mathcal{M}\otimes E^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1])

is
\psi_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}
, where
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}
is the biextension of
(\mathcal{M},E^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
given by
(\mathcal{P}_{1},\sigma)
. To conclude, it is enough to show that
\psi_{\tilde{\mathcal{P}}}
is also the image of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
via

\Hom(\mathcal{M}/T\otimes^{L}E^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1])\rightarrow\Hom(\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}E^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]).

By definition
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is the pullback of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}
along
E\times E^{*}\to A\times E^{*}
, hence the result follows again by functoriality of
equation

A.10
.

The proof of b) is very similar to its analogue in
Lemma

A.25
. We observe that the composition
T\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*}\overset{\psi_{\mathcal{P}}}{\rightarrow}\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]
is given by
\psi_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}}
. Here
\overline{\mathcal{P}}
is the biextension of
(T,\mathcal{M}^{*})
by
\mathbbm{G}_{m}
defined by

(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{A\times E^{*}}T\times E^{*},\tau_{T\times Y^{*}}).

We need to show that
\psi_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}}
is the image of
\gamma_{T}
via the morphism

\Hom(T\otimes^{L}Y^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m})\rightarrow\Hom(T\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]).

The map
T\rightarrow A
is trivial, hence
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{A\times E^{*}}T\times E^{*}\rightarrow T\times E^{*}
is isomorphic to the trivial biextension. Using the identification of
Remark

A.20
, c), we conclude that
\psi_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}}
is the image of
C_{\tau}:T\times Y^{*}\to\mathbbm{G}_{m}
via

\Hom(T\otimes^{L}Y^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m})\rightarrow\Hom(T\otimes^{L}\mathcal{M}^{*},\mathbbm{G}_{m}[1]).

We conclude since
C_{\tau}=\gamma_{T}
by
Remark

A.27
.
∎

A.4.2
The condensed Poincaré biextension

By
Corollary

A.21

\mathcal{P}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
is a biextension of
(\mathcal{M}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}},\mathcal{M}^{*}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}})
by
\overline{L}^{\times}
. We call it
condensed
Poincaré biextension. It is given by:

•

the biextension
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\overline{L})_{\mathrm{cond}}
of
